# Linux Syscall Compliance Matrix

Primary source: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/master/arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` (`arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl`, native `common`/`64` rows only; `x32` rows excluded).
Local cross-check: `/usr/src/kernels/6.19.6-100.fc42.x86_64/arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl`.

Generation rule: syscall numbers, ABI tags, names, and Linux entry points come from Linux source. Oxide source is used only for current-route annotation and subsystem impact mapping.

Generated rows: 385. Current main annotation: `2048296e0` (`B1245-sioc-ifmetric`).
Local cross-check delta: missing-from-local=471:rseq_slice_yield; extra-local=none.

## Status Legend

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `NEEDS-AUDIT` | Linux has a real entry; Oxide has a visible route, but semantic parity is not proven in this matrix. |
| `PARTIAL` | A committed fix/audit exists for part of the Linux contract, but the row still has remaining semantic or harness gaps. |
| `IMPL` | Full Linux-row semantics are implemented and backed by the required harness evidence. |
| `DISPATCH-GAP` | Linux has a real entry; Oxide lacks a visible `NR_*` constant or dispatch route. |
| `LINUX-ENOSYS` | Linux source intentionally has no real native implementation for this slot. Oxide must return `-ENOSYS`, not a stub success. |
| `IN-PROGRESS` | Local uncommitted work is currently touching this syscall family. |

## Audit Rules

| Rule | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Row authority | Add/remove/renumber rows only by re-reading Linux `syscall_64.tbl`. |
| Semantic authority | Verify behavior against Linux implementation source named in `Linux refs`, not against local notes. |
| Harness authority | A row is not complete until its required harness covers success, error ordering, permissions, ownership, namespace, symlink/hardlink, tmpfs/dev/proc/sysfs, and user-copy fault cases relevant to its systems. |
| No fallback truth | Oxide route presence only means code is reachable; it is not evidence of Linux compatibility. |

| `16:ioctl` | `IN-PROGRESS` | Socket-fd SIOC dispatch uses the socket-captured network namespace and owner-user-namespace `CAP_NET_ADMIN`; `SIOCADDRT`/`SIOCDELRT` validate Linux `rtentry`, mutate the canonical FIB atomically, and return `EEXIST`/`ESRCH`; `SIOCGIFCONF` enumerates namespace-owned IPv4 addresses without fixed ifindex scans. B1091 adds namespace-scoped security admission before INET/VSOCK queue-count ioctl state access; B1092 adds the same admission before netlink queue-count ioctl state access; B1093 adds deterministic namespace/operation counter and teardown-cleanup evidence; B1097 adds policy admission before `SIOCGSKNS` and interface `SIOC*` dispatch; B1098 adds canonical `NetDev::set_mtu` and Linux `ndo_change_mtu` delegation for `SIOCSIFMTU`; B1099 adds canonical `NetDev::set_mac` and Linux `ndo_set_mac_address` delegation for `SIOCSIFHWADDR`; B1100 adds canonical `tx_queue_len` ownership for `SIOCGIFTXQLEN`/`SIOCSIFTXQLEN`; B1101 replaces raw `ifreq` imports with fault-recoverable shared uaccess; B1102 converts shared IPv4 sockaddr output to fault-recoverable copyout; B1103 converts `SIOCGIFCONF` variable-length input/output to fault-recoverable uaccess; B1104 converts all fixed-field `ifreq` input/output, including `SIOCGIFNAME`, to fault-recoverable uaccess; B1117 purges namespace-scoped network security hooks and counters during canonical namespace teardown; B1120 adds canonical IPv4 broadcast ownership and SIOCSIFBRDADDR mutation with explicit readback; B1138 corrects native x86_64/aarch64 `struct ifreq` to 40 bytes and emits the complete SIOCGIFCONF record stride; B1139 restores explicit ENOTTY handling for SIOCATMARK in filesystem ioctl owners. | Direct syscall-context fault/order coverage; broader command, compat, and security policy differential coverage. |
| `41-55` INET socket family | existing row status retained | Socket-owned IPv4/IPv6 UDP/TCP, multicast memberships, PMTU/errors, implicit bind, diagnostics, send routing, and receive demux carry the captured or ingress-derived network namespace. IPv4/IPv6 fragment keys include namespace identity. `/proc/net/*`, rtnetlink dumps/mutations/multicast, and inet-diag use per-open or socket-captured namespace state. Hosted evidence: `net` 423, `netlink` 59, `nscg` 10, `procfs` 44, complete `syscalls` test set. B1121 adds TCP URG sequence/byte capture at the protocol owner; B1122 adds canonical SO_OOBINLINE option state and accepted-TCP inheritance; B1123 adds TCP `recvmsg(MSG_OOB)` copy-before-consume, `MSG_PEEK`, and urgent readiness; B1124 adds TCP `MSG_OOB` send with one-byte validation, URG pointer emission, and retransmission retention; B1125 adds canonical TCP `SIOCATMARK` and stream-read sequence tracking; B1126 adds SO_OOBINLINE-aware normal receive boundaries and OOB queue removal; B1127 preserves OOB consumption across out-of-order promotion; B1128 adds GNU/glibc TCP OOB differential records; B1129 routes ordinary recvfrom/read through the same inline-aware boundary; B1143 drains and closes live IPv4/IPv6 UDP and TCP transport objects before namespace table removal, and late socket release cannot recreate a removed table; B1144 closes live raw IPv4/IPv6 endpoints during the same teardown and makes late raw release tolerate a dead owner; B1145 closes AF_PACKET sockets, fanout, memberships, and rings during namespace teardown and wakes packet readers/pollers; B1146 asserts the packet poll-subscription generation advances during namespace teardown; B1147 makes IPv4/IPv6 UDP deactivation wake blocked readers and notify poll observers; B1148 makes TCP listener close notify poll observers after accept admission closes; B1149 makes established TCP close notify poll observers with terminal readiness. | Canonical refcounted network-namespace ownership and last-reference teardown remains a separate architecture change; B1143-B1149 focused teardown coverage passes, while kernel-target blocked-reader/epoll scheduling, multicast, interface removal, Oxide boot comparison for TCP OOB, and broader Linux differential coverage remain open. |

| 0 | common | `read` | `sys_read` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, procfs, sched, socket, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:23` | `IMPL` | `B719-syscall-000-read-complete; B853-vsock-final-file-release` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/read_write.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(read), fs/read_write.c:ksys_read, fs/read_write.c:vfs_read, fs/read_write.c:rw_verify_area, include/linux/fs.h:MAX_RW_COUNT, net/socket.c:sock_read_iter, net/socket.c:sock_recvmsg`; B854-socket-file-ownership; B868-network-blocked-io-close | `crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_read_shape.rs; vfs::file_read; net hosted suite; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks` | Completed Linux `read(2)` audit against local Linux source. Syscall fd lookup now precedes user-buffer validation; no-current/no-fd-table/bad-fd return `EBADF` before `EFAULT`; `FMODE_READ` rejects write-only fds before touching the user buffer; zero-length reads still validate fd/mode and call the backend; original count is user-access validated before Linux `MAX_RW_COUNT` clamp; backend sees the clamped count; negative-count-shaped u64 counts fault through access validation before clamp, matching Linux access_ok-before-rw_verify ordering on x86_64. VSOCK read now stays on the originally pinned File through VFS dispatch, so fd close/reuse cannot redirect the operation. Netlink read stays on the netlink receive queue path. Socket VFS read no longer returns blanket `EINVAL`: stream sockets use stream readers, datagram/packet sockets consume one message through the same receive core as `recvfrom(..., src=NULL)`, and fresh/listening TCP returns `ENOTCONN` like Linux. VFS read hooks still emit access notification for positive reads; syscall-level task I/O accounting now updates `rchar`/`syscr`, and procfs exposes dynamic `/proc/<pid>/io` plus `/proc/self/io`. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_read_shape`; `cargo test -p vfs file_read`; `cargo test -p net`; `cargo check -p fs`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. B854 keeps NETLINK read on the same pinned File through queue dispatch and proves close/reuse cannot retarget the operation. B868 adds lock-coupled terminal receive waits for VSOCK, NETLINK, and AF_UNIX while retaining the original File across close/reuse. |

Syscall fd lookup precedes user-buffer validation; no-current/no-fd-table/bad-fd return `EBADF` before `EFAULT`; `FMODE_WRITE` rejects read-only/O_PATH fds before touching the buffer; zero-length writes validate fd/mode and call the backend; original count is user-access validated before Linux `MAX_RW_COUNT` clamp; backend sees the clamped count. Source buffer now prevalidates a readable VMA to avoid kernel page faults until extable copy-from-user recovery exists; target checks compile the real path. Prior B715 work completed RLIMIT_FSIZE, `s_maxbytes`, socket, datagram, IPv6 UDP, AF_UNIX, and netlink write parity. Legacy `open` delegates to shared `openat(AT_FDCWD, path, flags, mode)` like Linux `do_sys_open`; prior B716 work completed `build_open_how` legacy masking, `O_PATH` stripping, `O_TMPFILE`, `O_CREAT|O_DIRECTORY`, `O_DIRECTORY` lookup, sync flag normalization, existing `O_CREAT` directory `EISDIR`, `OPENAT2_REGULAR`, and sticky-dir existing-target checks. This branch fixed the remaining fd-install path to match Linux `FD_ADD`: reserve fd with `get_unused_fd_flags` before open side effects, publish with `fd_install`, rollback with `put_unused_fd` on every failure, and return `EMFILE` before `f_op->open`/truncate side effects. It also removed the syscall-layer pre-call to `inode->i_fop->open`; `install_open_at` now owns the single Linux `do_dentry_open` open hook, skips it for `O_PATH`, exposes the final `File` to `on_open_file`, and propagates `O_TRUNC` backend errors before fd publication. Shared pathname fetch preserves Linux `EFAULT`/`ENOENT`/`ENAMETOOLONG` and byte-count semantics through `read_user_path` and VFS path gates. | 7 | common | `poll` | `sys_poll` | vfs | fd-table, fs-special, poll, rlimit, signal, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs, waitqueue | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:42` | `IMPL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/select.c:860, fs/select.c:872, fs/select.c:974, fs/select.c:1060, include/uapi/asm-generic/poll.h:11`; B854-socket-file-ownership | `fd-vfs-io-model; crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_poll_shape.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/tests/poll_syscall_shape.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/tests/poll_subs_mask.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/tests/poll_subs_exclusive.rs` | Audited against Linux `do_pollfd`/`do_sys_poll`/`SYSCALL_DEFINE3(poll)`: `nfds` is bounded by the current task's `RLIMIT_NOFILE`, user pollfds are copied in before fd-table lookup, only `revents` are copied out after polling, copyout faults surface after readiness evaluation, negative fds are ignored, non-negative bad fds report `POLLNVAL`, requested events are filtered while `POLLERR/HUP/NVAL` remain always-reportable, and `poll(NULL, 0, -1)` parks until a deliverable signal instead of returning immediately. Direct hosted syscall harness covers no-current/rlimit ordering, copyin-before-fdtable, missing fd-table `EBADF`, delayed copyout, copyout `EFAULT`, bad/negative fd behavior, timeout-zero, and zero-fd indefinite wait interrupted by signal; VFS harnesses cover readiness masks and subscriber wake semantics. B854 snapshots every initially resolved File once; subscriptions, readiness rescans, and source deadlines use only retained Files, preserving negative, duplicate, and `POLLNVAL` entries across close/reuse. F737 moves the shared poll engine onto an absolute monotonic end_time and onto `crate::pselect_ppoll::wait_verdict`, Linux `do_poll`'s break order: readiness, then a deliverable signal, then the expired deadline, so `poll(fds, n, 0)` with a pending signal is EINTR as `count = -ERESTARTNOHAND` makes it. `signal_pending` is `Task::deliverable_signals`, the shared `sig_ignored`-filtered rule. `revents` are copied out unconditionally after `do_poll` returns, so an interrupted poll zeroes them as Linux does. F743 completes the restart half. `do_poll` now ends an interrupted scan with Linux's `-ERESTARTNOHAND` (`fs/select.c:936-940`) instead of folding it to `-EINTR` inside the engine, and `SYSCALL_DEFINE3(poll)`'s tail (`fs/select.c:1074-1087`) converts that into an armed `do_restart_poll` block plus `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK`. poll(2) is the one member of this family that needs a block: `int timeout_msecs` cannot carry the residual timeout back the way ppoll's timespec does, so a restart without one would sleep the full duration again. The block stores ufds, nfds, has_timeout and the ABSOLUTE end_time, and the continuation re-arms itself exactly as `do_restart_poll` does. Slot 219 dispatches it. Hosted: `fs::sys_poll_shape` covers the armed payload for both the infinite and the finite timeout. |

B775 fixes ext4 project-id i_version parity: project-id changes through FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR now force an in-core i_version bump in ext4_fileattr_setproject after the Linux ext4_ioctl_setflags bump, matching ext4_ioctl_setproject's inode_inc_iversion path. Harness first failed with one bump and now proves the Linux two-bump path on the image-backed ext4 mount. B774 fixes ext4 filesystem label ioctls: `FS_IOC_GETFSLABEL`/`FS_IOC_SETFSLABEL` now route through filesystem `unlocked_ioctl`, use Linux request numbers, copy the ext4 17-byte label interface, enforce `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` before `SETFSLABEL` usercopy, reject labels without a NUL in the first 17 bytes, zero-pad and journal `s_volume_name`, restamp the superblock checksum, and expose same-mount plus remount-visible labels. The image harness proves in-core ctime, on-disk ctime, remount persistence, and i_version advance. Evidence: `cargo test -p ext4 --test fs_ioc_flags_image -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo check -p ext4`; `cargo check -p fs`; `git diff --check`; `./crates/kernel/syscalls/vdso/build.sh` (x86_64 built; aarch64 skipped absent toolchain); `cargo check -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec`. B772 fixes ext4 version ioctl family: generic ioctl routing now calls snapshotted `file_operations->unlocked_ioctl` before device fallbacks; ext4 regular and non-regular file ops implement `EXT4_IOC_GETVERSION`/legacy `FS_IOC_GETVERSION` by returning on-disk `i_generation`, and `EXT4_IOC_SETVERSION`/legacy `FS_IOC_SETVERSION` by preserving Linux admission/order: owner/CAP_FOWNER check, metadata_csum `ENOTTY`, mount write hold in the syscall ABI layer, user int copyin, journaled `i_generation`+ctime persistence, and in-core i_version bump. Evidence: `cargo test -p ext4 --test version_ioctl_image -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p ext4 --test fs_ioc_flags_image -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo check -p vfs`; `cargo check -p ext4`; `cargo check -p fs`; `./crates/kernel/syscalls/vdso/build.sh` (x86_64 built; aarch64 skipped absent toolchain); `cargo check -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec`; aarch64 target check remains blocked by missing `crates/kernel/syscalls/vdso/vdso-aarch64.so` because the local aarch64 VDSO cross compiler is absent. B771 fixes Linux generic fileattr casefold translation: `FS_CASEFOLD_FL` now maps to `FS_XFLAG_CASEFOLD` through syscall-side `FS_IOC_FSGETXATTR`/`FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR` conversion and VFS `fileattr` helpers, matching Linux `fs/file_attr.c::fileattr_fill_flags`/`fileattr_fill_xflags`; generic `FileAttr::from_i_flags` also reports `S_CASEFOLD` as `FS_CASEFOLD_FL`. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p vfs --test fileattr_prepare -- --nocapture`; `cargo check -p vfs`; `cargo check -p fs`; `git diff --check`; `./crates/kernel/syscalls/vdso/build.sh` (x86_64 VDSO built; aarch64 skipped because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent); `cargo check -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec`. B770 fixes Linux ext4 project-inherit semantics surrounding `FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR`: `ext4_new_inode` now inherits `i_projid` and Linux `EXT4_FL_INHERITED` flags from project-inherit directories, and ext4 hardlink/rename/exchange reject cross-project moves into `PROJINHERIT` directories with `EXDEV`, matching `fs/ext4/ialloc.c` and `fs/ext4/namei.c`. Evidence: `cargo test -p ext4 --test fs_ioc_flags_image -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p vfs --test fileattr_prepare -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p ext4 --lib -- --nocapture`; `cargo check -p ext4`; `cargo check -p fs`; `git diff --check`; `./crates/kernel/syscalls/vdso/build.sh` (x86_64 VDSO built; aarch64 skipped because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent); `cargo check -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec`. B769 fixes Linux queue-count ioctl parity: regular-file `FIONREAD` stays `i_size - f_pos`, while non-char `FIONREAD`/`SIOCOUTQ` now routes through `vfs::IoctlIntCmd` and `FileOps::ioctl_int` instead of a generic `poll()` boolean/zero fallback. Pipe/FIFO `FIONREAD` reports queued bytes; inet/unix/packet sockets, netlink, and vsock report backend queue counts where the local model has them; unsupported backends return `ENOTTY` before copyout. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_queue_count_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs pipe -- --nocapture`; `cargo check -p vfs`; `cargo check -p net --features hosted`; `cargo check -p netlink`; `cargo check -p fs`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 VDSO remains blocked by missing `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc`. B768 fixes Linux `fs/ioctl.c` `FIOASYNC` parity: `ioctl_fioasync` reads the user int, treats same-state requests as success, and changes `FASYNC` only through the open file's snapshotted `file_operations::fasync`; unsupported state changes now return `ENOTTY` without changing flags or generic-registering async delivery. VFS now owns a real `FileOps::fasync_file` hook, with pipe/FIFO and socket/netlink/vsock backends opting into the generic fasync registry. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_fasync_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_fowner -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs pipe -- --nocapture`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 target check remains blocked before this patch by missing `vdso-aarch64.so` because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent. B767 fixes Linux `fs/ioctl.c` default-arm regular-file gate for `FIBMAP` and legacy `FS_IOC_*SP` preallocation ioctls: only regular files now take the regular-file `file_ioctl` path; directories, symlinks, and other non-regular files fall through without usercopy or backend `bmap`/`fallocate` side effects. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_regular_gate_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 target check remains blocked before this patch by missing `vdso-aarch64.so` because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent. B766 fixes Linux `FIOQSIZE` parity from `fs/ioctl.c`: accepted directories/regular files/symlinks now return allocated inode bytes (`i_blocks * 512`, matching local `inode_get_bytes` shape) rather than logical size, with hosted coverage for regular sparse-shaped files, directories, symlinks, and special-file `ENOTTY` without user copyout. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_fioqsize_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p vfs --test fileattr_prepare -- --nocapture`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 target check remains blocked before this patch by missing `vdso-aarch64.so` because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent. B765 fixes ext4 fileattr admission from Linux `ext4_fileattr_set`: unsupported translated flags are rejected before the backend masks to user-modifiable bits, so unsupported DAX through `FS_IOC_SETFLAGS`/`FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR` no longer succeeds silently or mutates nothing. B764 fixes ext4 fileattr parity from Linux `ext4_ioctl_check_immutable` and `EXT4_FL_USER_VISIBLE`: immutable ext4 inodes now reject other flag/project-id changes while immutable remains set, `EXT4_EXTENTS_FL` is visible through `FS_IOC_GETFLAGS`, normal flag updates preserve it, and unsupported extent-layout toggles fail instead of clearing the on-disk extent bit. B763 adds Linux-shaped ext4 PROJECT RO_COMPAT admission plus journaled `i_projid` and ctime persistence for `FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR` on PROJECT filesystems, with in-memory PROJECT-bit image harness coverage. Prior B734-B744 covered common VFS dispatch, fileattr, remap admission, ext4 no-remap proof, and tty exclusive ioctls. Remaining before `IMPL`: filesystem-specific DAX/casefold support where supported by the backing filesystem, filesystem-specific remap backends only for filesystems that actually advertise Linux remap/reflink support, and remaining driver/file-specific ioctl families. STATUS CORRECTED IN-PROGRESS->PARTIAL (D394): the branch named in the Branch column is merged and deleted, so the work landed long ago. IN-PROGRESS means 'local uncommitted work is currently touching this syscall' -- leaving it set on merged work tells the next agent a live lane owns this row, which is exactly how duplicate lanes get opened. PARTIAL is the honest reading: a committed fix exists, full Linux parity is not proven. |

Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_select_shape -- --nocapture` (8/8); `cargo test -p fs --test sys_poll_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p vfs --test poll_syscall_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zbuild-std-features=compiler-builtins-mem -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. B854 snapshots each selected File once; subscriptions, readiness rescans, and deadlines cannot be redirected by concurrent close/reuse. `pselect6` uses the same corrected engine. F737 moves the shared select engine onto `crate::pselect_ppoll::wait_verdict`, Linux `do_select` plus `core_sys_select` break order, and onto `Task::deliverable_signals` for `signal_pending`; the timeval writeback now goes through the shared `poll_select_finish` rule, honouring the `STICKY_TIMEOUTS` persona and the zero-timeout no-update case. F743: `do_select`/`core_sys_select` end an interrupted wait with `-ERESTARTNOHAND` (`fs/select.c:681-686`) rather than a folded `-EINTR`, so the no-handler cases Linux restarts (SIG_DFL, SIG_IGN, a job-control stop) restart here too; the timeval writeback that makes that restart correct already runs. select(2) arms no restart block, matching `kern_select`. The `sticky:` fold (`fs/select.c:361-363`) is now applied: `-ERESTARTNOHAND` becomes `-EINTR` exactly when the residual timeout could not reach userspace (STICKY_TIMEOUTS persona or a faulting copyout), because a call whose timeout was not updated cannot be restarted. Owned by the shared `pselect_ppoll::{wait_verdict, timeout_writeback_plan, finish_return}` rules. |

F743 removes the private signal triage: `pause_wake` was one of three divergent copies of the same rule. All three now call `Task::sleep_wake`, the single owner beside `Task::deliverable_signals`, so pause, nanosleep and clock_nanosleep cannot drift. | 35 | common | `nanosleep` | `sys_nanosleep` | time | sched, sched-clock, signals, syscall-abi, time, timers, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:76` | `IMPL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl:47, kernel/time/hrtimer.c:2371-2480` | `fs::sys_nanosleep_shape; syscall::restart; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks` | Completed Linux `nanosleep(2)` audit against upstream `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(nanosleep)`/`hrtimer_nanosleep`/`do_nanosleep`/`nanosleep_copyout`: request timespec is copied/validated before any `rem` access; invalid user request returns `EFAULT`, invalid `{tv_sec,tv_nsec}` returns `EINVAL`, zero duration succeeds without touching `rem`, relative sleeps use monotonic deadlines, the kernel path parks on a scheduler wait list with an absolute wake deadline instead of spinning runnable, interruption is gated on actionable deliverable signals rather than raw pending bits, ignored/default-no-op signals are discarded, default stop signals stop/resume the wait, masked signals do not interrupt, SIGKILL/SIGSTOP bypass masks, interrupted sleeps copy remaining time only when `rem` is non-null and time remains, bad `rem` faults only at interruption copyout, and successful interruption returns internal Linux `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK` which the dispatch boundary normalizes to user-visible `EINTR`. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_nanosleep_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p syscall restart -- --nocapture`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. F743: the sleep engine is now shared with `clock_nanosleep(2)` (230) and parameterised by `HRTIMER_MODE_ABS` vs `_REL`, so both slots use one park loop, one triage and one interrupted tail. `nanosleep(2)` itself is always `HRTIMER_MODE_REL` + CLOCK_MONOTONIC (`kernel/time/hrtimer.c:2480`), so it always takes the copy-out-and-arm arm. The private `sleep_wake` copy is gone in favour of `Task::sleep_wake`. That unification exposed a real hole in `Task::deliverable_signals`, fixed here: it returned early when `pending & !sigmask` was zero, which hid a MASKED SIGKILL/SIGSTOP and left a fully-masked sleeper unkillable; Linux can never have those two in `blocked` (`__set_task_blocked` -> `sigdelsetmask`), so the override is now explicit, matching `signum::next_deliverable`. Hosted: `fs::sys_nanosleep_shape` grew coverage for the ABS/REL split, the remainder-resume across two interruptions, and the ignored-signal rule; `sched::task::sigwake` adds 8 triage tests. |

Previously the `semflg` argument was DISCARDED ENTIRELY: no `IPC_CREAT`/`IPC_EXCL`, no mode bits, no credentials, and every key lookup silently returned or created regardless of permission. Now full Linux key rules: `IPC_PRIVATE` always creates; existing key with `IPC_CREAT\|IPC_EXCL` is `EEXIST`; `nsems` greater than the existing set is `EINVAL` (`sem_more_checks`); `ipcperms(semflg)` gates an existing set with `EACCES`; a missing key without `IPC_CREAT` is `ENOENT`. Create path enforces `nsems == 0` -> `EINVAL`, `nsems < 0 \|\| > SEMMSL` -> `EINVAL` ahead of everything, and `used_sems + nsems > SEMMNS` -> `ENOSPC` (per-namespace `used_sems` accounting did not exist before, so `SEMMNS` was unenforceable). mode is `semflg & S_IRWXUGO`, uid=cuid=euid, gid=cgid=egid, values zeroed, `sem_ctime` stamped. Shared substrate landed with this branch: `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv/` now carries one `ipcperms()`/`ipc_update_perm()` (`perm.rs`, also adopted by `sysv_shm`, deleting its private copy), the real per-namespace cyclic idx+seq identifier space (`ids.rs`, so a stale id whose slot was recycled fails `EINVAL` per `ipc_checkid`), and the `ipc64_perm`/`semid64_ds`/`msqid64_ds`/`seminfo`/`msginfo` byte layouts (`uapi.rs`). `semid64_ds` is arch-divergent and is cfg-gated: x86 interleaves an unused word after `sem_otime` and `sem_ctime` (104 bytes) where asm-generic/aarch64 does not (88 bytes) — a single layout would have handed aarch64 userspace a `sem_nsems` read out of padding. |

Fixed: `sem_op == 0` (wait-for-zero) was treated as a no-op that always committed instead of blocking until `semval == 0`; **`SEM_UNDO` did not exist at all** and is now real (per-process `semadj[nsems]` keyed by thread group, `semadj -= sem_op` with the `[-(SEMAEM+1), SEMAEM]` bound -> `ERANGE`, rolled back with the rest of a non-committing batch, applied and clamped to `[0, SEMVMX]` by `exit_sem` at process exit and invalidated by `IPC_RMID`); errno ordering corrected to E2BIG (`nsops > SEMOPM`) -> EINVAL (`nsops < 1`) -> EFAULT -> EFBIG (`max sem_num >= nsems`, previously EINVAL) -> EACCES -> EIDRM; `IPC_NOWAIT` now honours the BLOCKING sop rather than "any sop"; `alter` is computed so a pure wait-for-zero batch needs only `S_IRUGO`; `semtimedop` honoured its timeout not at all and now converts to an absolute deadline (`EAGAIN` on expiry); `EINTR` on a signalled wake; `sempid`/`semncnt`/`semzcnt` now tracked. Only the incremental (`perform_atomic_semop_slow`) form is implemented, deliberately: the two-pass fast path is wrong for duplicated `sem_num` (which is why Linux routes `dupsop` away from it) and is a write-avoidance optimisation, not a semantic. `exit_sem` is wired into both the syscall exit path (`060_exit.rs`, guarded on the last thread of the group) and the fatal-exit path (`zombies.rs`, via a new `set_sysvsem_exit_hook` mirroring the robust-futex hook, since `sched` cannot depend on `ipc`). NOT VERIFIED: the sleeping path has never executed. Hosted `block::park_until` is a stub returning `Wake::Signal`, so tests prove only that the retry loop terminates with `EINTR`; real park/wake, `EIDRM` on a removal landing while parked, and timeout expiry are covered by inspection against `ipc/sem.c` / `ipc/msg.c` only. Boot smoke reaches `basic.target` but glibc/systemd does not exercise SysV blocking during boot. `SEM_UNDO` sharing is keyed by thread group, which matches Linux for every real caller (glibc always passes `CLONE_SYSVSEM` from `pthread_create` and never from `fork`) but is not exact for a hand-rolled `clone(CLONE_SYSVSEM)` without `CLONE_THREAD`; documented at the module head. The `un->semid == -1` `EIDRM` branch (an `IPC_RMID` landing inside one `semop` between `find_alloc_undo` and the set lock) is correct by inspection but unreachable from a hosted test. | 67 | common | `shmdt` | `sys_shmdt` | ipc | ipc, ipc-namespace, permissions, sched, syscall-abi, usercopy, vmm | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:64` | `PARTIAL` | `F728-sysv-ipc-audit` | `ipc/shm.c:ksys_shmdt, ipc/shm.c:shm_close, ipc/shm.c:shm_destroy` | `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv/{sem,msg}/tests/*.rs`; `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv/ids/tests.rs`; `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv_shm/shmdt/tests.rs`; `cargo test -p ipc` (153 lib + 13 integration); `cargo test -p sched` (251); `cargo test -p syscalls` (188); x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds; `tools/boot-smoke.sh x86` | Completed `shmdt(2)` audit against `ksys_shmdt`. Previously it unmapped exactly `PAGE_SIZE` at the given address, returned 0 for ANY page-aligned address, and never touched `shm_nattch` — so a multi-page segment was never fully detached (the comment admitted it and deferred the rest to execve/exit), an address that was not an attachment reported a successful detach that had not happened, and an `IPC_RMID`-marked segment was never destroyed. Now implements the real algorithm: locate the anchor VMA whose file offset equals its distance from `shmaddr` (Linux `(vm_start - addr)/PAGE_SIZE == vm_pgoff`), take the attachment extent from the SEGMENT rather than the anchor (the anchor may be the surviving fragment of a partially unmapped attach), sweep the remaining same-segment fragments inside that extent, `EINVAL` when no anchor exists, then run `shm_close` accounting so the last detach destroys a segment already marked `SHM_DEST`. Geometry is factored into a pure function over VMA descriptors and covered hosted. NOT VERIFIED: the address-space half (`snapshot_vmas` + `munmap`) is not exercised by any test — hosted builds have no `mm` — and no booted program calls `shmdt`. KNOWN REMAINING GAP: `shm_nattch` is decremented by `shmdt` but NOT by address-space teardown at `exit`/`execve`, where Linux decrements via `shm_close` on VMA destruction; a process that exits without detaching still leaks the attach count and can keep an `IPC_RMID`'d segment alive. That belongs to the VMA-lifecycle owner, not slot 67. Fixed: `mtype < 1` was accepted and is now `EINVAL`; the per-message cap was 4096 and is now `MSGMAX` (8192); queue capacity was "16 messages" and is now Linux's byte-based `msg_fits_inqueue`, BOTH halves (`msgsz + q_cbytes <= q_qbytes && 1 + q_qnum <= q_qbytes`, the count half covered by a dedicated zero-length-message test); no permission check existed and `ipcperms(S_IWUGO)` now applies; user I/O was raw unvalidated pointer dereference and now goes through a real VMA-walk validation (`EFAULT`); `q_lspid`/`q_stime`/`q_cbytes`/`q_qnum` accounting did not exist; `EINTR` on a signalled wake. Stale ids are now rejected by `ipc_checkid` sequence validation instead of a linear match on the raw id. NOT VERIFIED: the sleeping path has never executed. Hosted `block::park_until` is a stub returning `Wake::Signal`, so tests prove only that the retry loop terminates with `EINTR`; real park/wake, `EIDRM` on a removal landing while parked, and timeout expiry are covered by inspection against `ipc/sem.c` / `ipc/msg.c` only. Boot smoke reaches `basic.target` but glibc/systemd does not exercise SysV blocking during boot. Linux's `pipelined_send` targeted hand-off is deliberately NOT reproduced: `WaitList` cannot wake one specific task, so a `wake_one` to a non-matching receiver would hang. The message is always enqueued and all receivers are woken to re-run `find_msg` under the lock, reaching the same observable state; the transient difference is that `q_cbytes`/`q_qnum` briefly count a message Linux would have handed straight over. THE WORST BUG IN THIS LANE: an oversized message was SILENTLY TRUNCATED (`min(msgsz, len)`) and reported as success — silent data loss where Linux errors. Now `bufsz < m_ts && !MSG_NOERROR` is `E2BIG` with the message LEFT ON THE QUEUE (asserted by test), and `MSG_NOERROR` truncates, removes, and returns `bufsz`. `MSG_NOERROR`, `MSG_EXCEPT` and `MSG_COPY` did not exist at all and are implemented, including `MSG_COPY`'s index semantics, its non-removal, its `EINVAL` without `IPC_NOWAIT` or with `MSG_EXCEPT`, and the `copy_msg` `EINVAL` only reachable with `MSG_NOERROR`. Type selection was also wrong: for `msgtyp < 0` the old code took the FIRST match, where Linux `find_msg` narrows `msgtyp` to `m_type - 1` on each `SEARCH_LESSEQUAL` hit and keeps scanning, yielding the LOWEST type with a FIFO tie-break and an immediate return on `m_type == 1`; both the tie case and the `LONG_MIN -> LONG_MAX` edge are tested. A bogus `bufsz > 4096 -> EINVAL` was removed (Linux caps only `MSG_COPY`). Added `ipcperms(S_IRUGO)`, validated user I/O, `q_lrpid`/`q_rtime`/`q_cbytes`/`q_qnum` accounting, `ENOMSG` on `IPC_NOWAIT`, and `EINTR`. NOT VERIFIED: the sleeping path has never executed. Boot smoke reaches `basic.target` but glibc/systemd does not exercise SysV blocking during boot. All cmds now implemented against the real `msqid64_ds` (120 bytes; offsets re-derived from the headers and confirmed identical on x86_64 and aarch64) and `msginfo` layouts, with full `q_stime`/`q_rtime`/`q_ctime`/`q_cbytes`/`q_qnum`/`q_qbytes`/`q_lspid`/`q_lrpid` reporting (pids are TGIDs per `task_tgid`). `IPC_STAT` returns 0 while `MSG_STAT`/`MSG_STAT_ANY` address by raw INDEX and return the full id, `MSG_STAT_ANY` skipping the `S_IRUGO` check. `IPC_SET` takes `msg_qbytes` from the buffer, gates a raise above `MSGMNB` on `CAP_SYS_RESOURCE` (`EPERM`), requires `ipcctl_obtain_check` ownership, and wakes blocked receivers and senders per `msgctl_down`. `IPC_RMID` previously removed by id with NO namespace filter — a cross-namespace removal — and now runs `freeque` inside the owning namespace, waking every blocked peer to `EIDRM`. Deliberately reproduced Linux quirk: `ksys_msgctl` narrows `msqid64_ds.msg_qbytes` to `int` before both the `CAP_SYS_RESOURCE` gate and the store into `unsigned long q_qbytes`; pinned by test. | 72 | common | `fcntl` | `sys_fcntl` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:52` | `PARTIAL` | `B768-fasync-fileops-gate` | `fs/fcntl.c:do_fcntl, fs/file.c:f_dupfd`; B854-socket-file-ownership | `syscall-abi-model` | B768 fixes the `F_SETFL(O_ASYNC)` side of Linux `fs/fcntl.c::setfl`: changed async state calls backend `f_op->fasync` when present and no longer generic-registers unsupported files; absent `fasync` remains ignored as Linux does for `F_SETFL`, while backend errors abort before the final flag store. Evidence: x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; shared VFS/ioctl fasync harness in syscall 16 evidence. Fixed `F_DUPFD`/`F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC` to use the caller's `RLIMIT_NOFILE` soft limit and return `EINVAL` when the requested minimum is at/above that limit. Remaining: full command-by-command fcntl audit and hosted syscall-context harness. B854 makes `F_DUPFD` and `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC` install the already-pinned open file description; CLOEXEC is published atomically, and deterministic source close/reuse and reservation schedules pass. LEASE GAP (F750): `F_SETLEASE` registers a lease, but this kernel has NO BLOCKING LEASE BREAK. `vfs::file::lease_force_break` revokes every conflicting lease immediately and returns, where Linux `__break_lease` (`fs/locks.c:1664-1792`) BLOCKS the breaking opener in `wait_event_interruptible_timeout` until the holder downgrades or `break_time` expires, sends the holder its lease-break signal, and only then proceeds; `LEASE_BREAK_NONBLOCK` is the sole path that returns `-EWOULDBLOCK` (`:1743`) and an interrupted break is `-ERESTARTSYS`, never EINTR. So an open that should have waited for the lease holder proceeds instantly here and the holder is never given its grace period. No wait exists to classify, which is why the interruptible-wait sweep found nothing to change on this path. |

Ownership confirmed Linux-correct: the key is the `Arc<vfs::File>` address = `flc_owner = filp`, so `dup(2)` and `fork(2)` share one lock and only the final reference drop releases it (proved by `dup_shares_the_lock_because_the_description_is_the_owner`, `fork_inherits_the_same_lock_owner`, `last_reference_drop_releases_the_lock`). Four divergences FIXED: (1) `LOCK_MAND` returned `EINVAL`, Linux accepts and IGNORES it (`pr_warn_once` then `return 0`); (2) the operation validity check ran AFTER the fd lookup, so `flock(badfd, bogus_op)` reported `EBADF` where Linux reports `EINVAL` — `flock_translate_cmd` precedes `CLASS(fd, f)`; (3) no `f_mode` gate, so an `O_PATH` fd could take a lock — Linux returns `EBADF` when neither `FMODE_READ` nor `FMODE_WRITE` is set and the op is not `LOCK_UN`; (4) `File::Drop` released the flock but never woke contenders, so a task parked in blocking `flock(2)` slept forever when the holder closed — Linux `locks_delete_lock_ctx` calls `locks_wake_up_blocks` (regression-pinned by `last_reference_drop_wakes_parked_contenders`). Blocked-upgrade non-atomicity, `LOCK_NB`→`EWOULDBLOCK`, `EINTR`, and shared-lock stacking already matched. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_flock_shape` (10 passed); both-arch kernel builds. |
| 74 | common | `fsync` | `sys_fsync` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:104` | `IMPL` | `F726-fs-simple-audit`; `B1440-fsync-per-inode-not-syncfs` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/sync.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE1(fsync), fs/sync.c:do_fsync, fs/sync.c:vfs_fsync, fs/sync.c:vfs_fsync_range, fs/libfs.c:noop_fsync` | `crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_fsync_shape.rs` | Audited against `fs/sync.c`. Work moved out of the shim into `fs::sync::vfs_fsync`. Two divergences FIXED: (1) a description with no backing store returned SUCCESS — Linux `vfs_fsync_range` returns `EINVAL` when `!file->f_op->fsync`, which is the case for pipes/FIFOs (`pipefifo_fops`), sockets (`socket_file_ops`), the anon-inode fds (eventfd/epoll/timerfd/signalfd/inotify), and character devices (`memory_fops`, `tty_fops`); reporting durability for a descriptor with no backing store is a data-integrity lie. The decision lives in a `FileOps::fsync` slot (Linux `f_op->fsync`) whose generic default answers by description type and which any backend may override — no list kept at the syscall layer. (2) The metadata commit went through `ext4::commit_rootfs_journal()`, which only ever commits the ROOT mount's journal, so `fsync` on a file on any OTHER ext4 mount returned success without committing. **B1440 correction:** the first attempt at (2) routed the commit through the superblock's `sync_fs`, which is WRONG — `sync_fs` is the whole-mount pass behind `sync(2)`/`syncfs(2)` and ext4's writes back every dirty page on the filesystem plus a device flush, so every `fsync` cost a full `syncfs`. ext4 now installs its own `f_op->fsync` (`ext4_sync_file`) on BOTH its regular-file and directory operations, committing only the journal transaction carrying that inode, resolved from the inode's own `i_private` so a non-root mount is still durable. Causality-checked: restoring the `sync_fs` call fails `fsync_uses_the_per_inode_slot_and_never_the_whole_fs_pass` with 2 observed invocations vs the expected 0. Page-cache writeback via `i_mapping()->writeback()` retained; `EBADF` ordering already matched. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_fsync_shape` (4 passed). |

REMAINING (why PARTIAL, not IMPL): `fdatasync` does not yet SKIP the non-essential metadata Linux skips — the backend commits identically for both, so this is currently a correct SUPERSET (more durable than required, never less) rather than full Linux-row semantics. DOWNGRADED IMPL->PARTIAL (B1441): this row's own Evidence discloses that ETXTBSY on truncating a running executable is absent (no `i_writecount`/`deny_write_access` in-tree). | 78 | common | `getdents` | `sys_getdents` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/217_getdents64.rs:66` | `PARTIAL` | `F736-affinity-getdents-audit` | `fs/readdir.c, include/linux/dirent.h, include/linux/fs.h dir_emit_dots, fs/ext4/dir.c` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | Rewritten against `fs/readdir.c` `SYSCALL_DEFINE3(getdents)`/`filldir`. `linux_dirent` layout byte-exact (d_ino@0, d_off@8, d_reclen@16, d_name@18, d_type at reclen-1) and distinct from `linux_dirent64`; reclen = ALIGN(offsetof(d_name)+namlen+2, sizeof(long)). Return rule is Linux's tail: bytes already packed win over a late backend error, EINVAL only when the buffer cannot hold the FIRST record, 0 only at end-of-directory; `iterate_dir` stores f_pos error-or-not; last record's d_off sealed with the final ctx.pos; count truncated to unsigned int; ENOTDIR decided before any user access; `verify_dirent_name` EIO on empty/`/`/>=PATH_MAX names; signal_pending abandons the walk after the first record. `.`/`..` now emitted for EVERY filesystem (`vfs::readdir_dots`, cursors 0/1, child cookies shifted by 2) with an opt-out for ext4/FUSE which carry their own; before this they were missing on tmpfs, kernfs, devfs, devpts, procfs, sysfs, cgroupfs, tracefs, binfmt_misc and autofs. d_type is honest per backend: tmpfs/kernfs/procfs/sysfs/cgroupfs report the real per-inode type; ext4 decodes the on-disk file_type byte but only when EXT4_FEATURE_INCOMPAT_FILETYPE is set (otherwise DT_UNKNOWN, previously DT_REG for every entry including directories); FUSE passes the daemon's DT_* through including DT_UNKNOWN. ext4 readdir cookies are now byte offsets (Linux `ext4_readdir`) so lseek(d_off) is stable and one listing is O(N) not O(N^2), and a block read error or corrupt rec_len surfaces EIO instead of looking like end-of-directory. PARTIAL: the synthetic filesystems still use ordinal cursors, so an insert/unlink between two getdents calls shifts the cookies of later entries (Linux uses cursor dentries / stable hashes); the f_version stale-cursor restart limits this to a restart rather than a torn listing. Tests: syscalls::getdents_abi (20 hosted), crates/kernel/fs/tests/getdents_dots_tmpfs.rs (6, real tmpfs), crates/kernel/ext4/tests/getdents_dtype_image.rs (5, real ext4 image). |

| 95 | common | `umask` | `sys_umask` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:50` | `IMPL` | `F726-fs-simple-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE1(umask), include/linux/fs_struct.h` | `crates/kernel/sched/src/tests/umask.rs` | Audited against `kernel/sys.c` (`mask = xchg(&current->fs->umask, mask & S_IRWXUGO); return mask;`). Returning the PREVIOUS mask and masking to `0777` were already correct. Divergence FIXED: the mask lived on the TASK (`Task.umask`), not on the shared `fs_struct` owner — so threads of one process (`CLONE_FS`) each had a PRIVATE umask, and `umask(2)` in one thread was invisible to its siblings, while `unshare(CLONE_FS)` did not split it. It now lives in `FsContext` (oxide's `fs_struct`), so `CLONE_FS` siblings share one mask, `fork(2)` snapshot-copies it through `inherit_fs_context_from`, and `unshare(CLONE_FS)` detaches it; the explicit umask copy in `clone` is gone as redundant. All readers (`open`/`openat`/`mkdir`/`mkdirat`/`mknod`, `/proc/<pid>/status`) moved to `Task::umask()`. Evidence: `cargo test -p sched` (254 passed, incl. 3 new umask tests). |

| 97 | common | `getrlimit` | `sys_getrlimit` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:14` | `PARTIAL` | `F727-sysinfo-limits-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` | `syscall-abi-model` | Completed `getrlimit(2)` audit against /home/nd/oxide/linux-master kernel/sys.c `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(getrlimit)` -> `do_prlimit(current, resource, NULL, &value)`. Verified: `resource >= RLIM_NLIMITS` (16) returns EINVAL BEFORE the copy-out, so a bad index reports EINVAL even when `rlim` is unwritable (ordering was already correct, now documented + locked); `struct rlimit` is 2x u64; `RLIM_INFINITY` is `~0UL`; the 16 `RLIMIT_*` indices match asm-generic/resource.h; the init defaults match `INIT_RLIMITS` (_STK_LIM 8 MiB, NOFILE 1024/4096, CORE 0). FIXED divergence: `do_prlimit` reads `tsk->signal->rlim` -- rlimits are PROCESS-WIDE in Linux, shared by every thread of a thread group. Oxide held them per-`Task`, so a `CLONE_THREAD` sibling got a private copy and `getrlimit` answered STALE after another thread's `setrlimit`. The table moved to the shared `ThreadGroup` (Linux `signal_struct.rlim`); `fork(2)` still snapshots the parent's table into the child's fresh group (`copy_signal`). Also fixed a dual-truth bug this exposed: `/proc/self/limits` served a HARDCODED static blob that contradicted both the real defaults and `/proc/<pid>/limits` (it claimed `Max locked memory 65536`, `Max msgqueue size 819200`, `Max nice priority 0`); it now renders through the same live renderer, so `setrlimit` is visible through both. PARTIAL because the SYSCALL is complete but ENFORCEMENT is not: only FSIZE (SIGXFSZ+EFBIG), DATA (brk), NOFILE (every fd path), NICE and RTPRIO are enforced, plus STACK at execve only (the fault-time MAP_GROWSDOWN cap is a hardcoded 8 MiB constant, not the rlimit). STORED-BUT-UNENFORCED: CPU (no SIGXCPU anywhere), CORE (coredump.rs never reads it), NPROC (no fork EAGAIN), MEMLOCK, AS, SIGPENDING (RT queue uses a fixed cap), MSGQUEUE, RTTIME. RSS and LOCKS are also no-ops in upstream Linux, so the genuinely-missing set is CPU/CORE/NPROC/MEMLOCK/AS/SIGPENDING/MSGQUEUE/RTTIME -- each belongs to the lane that owns its enforcement point (fork, mmap, mlock, coredump, POSIX-CPU-timers, mqueue, RT throttle), not to this one. Evidence: `cargo test -p sched --lib rlimit_prio` (10 tests, incl. thread-group sharing, separate-process isolation, fork snapshot, all 16 indices + INIT_RLIMITS defaults). Both arches build. |

Fixed: PEEK now `put_user`s the word into `data` and returns 0 (was returning the word as the syscall rv, so any word with the sign bit set surfaced as a bogus errno through glibc); PEEK/POKE short foreign access is EIO not EFAULT; PEEKUSER/POKEUSER bound-check `struct user` (misaligned or >= 928 = EIO, out-of-range register write no longer fake-succeeds, DR writes EIO); GETREGS/SETREGS now build a real 27-quadword `user_regs_struct` from the entry frame (was a verbatim 15-slot frame copy = wrong field in every slot) and are EIO on aarch64 as in Linux; GETREGSET/SETREGSET now read the `struct iovec` from `data`, honour the NT_ type in `addr`, clamp and write back `iov_len` (previously treated `data` as the buffer, corrupting the tracer's iovec — and GETREGSET is arm64's only GP-register path); aarch64 frame base corrected 0xD0 -> the recorded `svc_frame`/0x120 (old offset read x19..x28 as x0..x17 and wrote 0x40 past the stack top); `__ptrace_may_access` now uses the caller's REAL uid/gid per PTRACE_MODE_ATTACH_REALCREDS and applies the dumpable gate; ATTACH refuses kthreads and exiting targets; TRACEME on an already-traced task is EPERM; CONT/SYSCALL/SINGLESTEP/DETACH validate the signal (EIO); SEIZE validates addr/options (EIO) vs SETOPTIONS (EINVAL) incl. SUSPEND_SECCOMP CAP_SYS_ADMIN; INTERRUPT/LISTEN require PT_SEIZED; GETSIGINFO/SETSIGINFO are EINVAL with no last_siginfo (was a synthesised SIGTRAP); GETSIGMASK/SETSIGMASK added; unknown request is EIO not EINVAL; syscall stops report `SIGTRAP\|0x80` under PTRACE_O_TRACESYSGOOD. Hosted tests: 37 in `s101_ptrace_{decide,perm,regs}`. NOT implemented, honestly EIO: PEEKSIGINFO, GET/SET_SYSCALL_INFO, SECCOMP_GET_FILTER/METADATA, GET_RSEQ_CONFIGURATION, SYSCALL_USER_DISPATCH_CONFIG, GETFDPIC; no hardware debug registers (DR0-7 read 0, write EIO); PTRACE_EVENT_* fork/clone/exec stops are not generated; Yama `ptrace_scope` not consulted; single-stop-per-request state machine not exercised under a real tracer (no boot verification in this lane). |

Returns `cred->uid` (real uid); no arguments, no error path. Covered by the transition harness, which asserts the real uid after every set*id form. REMAINING: user-namespace id translation (`make_kuid`/`from_kuid_munged`, `kernel/user_namespace.c`) is not modelled anywhere in-tree — every subsystem (chown/stat/getuid/setuid alike) operates on HOST ids, so inside a non-init user namespace this syscall accepts/returns host rather than namespace-relative ids. Closing it is a kernel-wide cred-model lane, not a cred-syscall-local fix. `ns_capable_setid` likewise degrades to a global effective-set check because capabilities are not per-user-namespace yet. |
| 103 | common | `syslog` | `sys_syslog` | security | procfs, security, syscall-abi, sysfs, system-control, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/103_syslog.rs` | IMPL | `F732-ptrace-syslog-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c, kernel/module/, kernel/reboot.c` | `cred-security-model` | Audited vs `kernel/printk/printk.c` `do_syslog`/`check_syslog_permissions` (linux-master v7.2.0-rc4). Work moved out of the ABI crate (`syscall::dmesg`, deleted) into `103_syslog.rs` + hosted-tested `103_syslog/decide.rs` per `53`. Hosted tests: 10 decide + 5 klog console-level. Divergence: console default loglevel is 8 (`CONSOLE_LOGLEVEL_DEBUG`) so existing kdebug boot output is unchanged until userspace lowers it; a blocked READ is woken by a 20ms deadline re-park rather than from the emit path (klog's `ring_push` runs in NMI/lock-held contexts, where Linux defers via irq_work); READ_ALL/SIZE_UNREAD are byte-granular, not record-granular; not boot-verified in this lane. |

FIXED: `(uid_t)-1` is now `EINVAL` (`uid_valid`) instead of being stored as a real uid; the unprivileged target set is now `{ruid, suid}` ONLY — the previous code also accepted the current EFFECTIVE uid, letting a task re-acquire an identity Linux forbids. `CAP_SETUID` sets real+saved+effective+fs; without it only effective+fs move. `cap_emulate_setxuid` runs on the resulting triple, and `commit_creds` downgrades dumpability + clears `pdeath_signal`. Closing it is a kernel-wide cred-model lane, not a cred-syscall-local fix. `ns_capable_setid` likewise degrades to a global effective-set check because capabilities are not per-user-namespace yet. |

| 109 | common | `setpgid` | `sys_setpgid` | sched | namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, tty, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:53` | `IMPL` | `F725-session-pgid-audit` | `kernel/sys.c:1114 SYSCALL_DEFINE2(setpgid)`, `include/linux/sched/signal.h same_thread_group`, `fs/exec.c begin_new_exec (PF_FORKNOEXEC)` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | Rewrote as an ABI shim over `sched::session::setpgid`. Was a bare `pgid.store` with NO validation. Now the full Linux ladder in Linux's order: `pgid<0` EINVAL (after the pid/pgid 0-aliases, so `setpgid(-5,0)` is EINVAL not ESRCH); unknown/negative pid ESRCH; non-thread-group-leader target EINVAL; child in a different session EPERM; child that already exec'd EACCES (Linux returns EACCES here, not EPERM) via the new `Task::forknoexec` (`PF_FORKNOEXEC`, set at fork, cleared in `reset_per_execve_state`); target neither self nor child ESRCH; session-leader target EPERM; destination pgrp absent or in another session EPERM. `pid==0` now resolves the caller's PROCESS (was the calling thread). pgid moved from `Task` to `ThreadGroup`, so a move applies to every thread of the process (Linux `task->signal`) instead of only the caller. Harness: 27 hosted tests in `crates/kernel/sched/src/tests/session.rs`; `cargo test -p sched` 282/282; x86_64+aarch64 kernel builds; `make smoke-x86`/`smoke-arm` both reach `basic.target`. |
| 110 | common | `getppid` | `sys_getppid` | sched | namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, tty, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:24` | `IMPL` | `F725-session-pgid-audit` | `kernel/sys.c:1016 SYSCALL_DEFINE0(getppid)` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | ABI shim over `sched::session::getppid` = Linux `task_tgid_vnr(real_parent)`. Resolves the parent's thread-group LEADER before taking the namespace-visible pid, so a child forked by a worker thread reports the parent PROCESS, not the parent thread; falls back to the process vpid when no namespace mapping exists (previously returned 0 in that case). Parent gone / outside the pid namespace still reports 0, as Linux does for pid 1. Harness: `tests/session.rs` getppid cases; `cargo test -p sched` 282/282; both arches build + boot. |

| 112 | common | `setsid` | `sys_setsid` | sched | namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, tty, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:54` | `PARTIAL` | `F725-session-pgid-audit` | `kernel/sys.c:1268 ksys_setsid`, `kernel/sys.c:1257 set_special_pids`, `drivers/tty/tty_jobctrl.c proc_clear_tty` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | ABI shim over `sched::session::setsid`. Was unconditional success. Now both Linux EPERM gates: already a session leader (new `ThreadGroup::session_leader`, Linux `signal_struct::leader`, latched atomically so a failed call never latches), and a process group already numbered with the caller's pid (`pid_task(sid, PIDTYPE_PGID)`) — which is the 'caller is already a process group leader' case. sid+pgid are set on the thread group, so every thread of the process joins the new session/group. REMAINING: the controlling terminal is still a per-`Task` field, so setsid clears only the calling thread's `ctty`; Linux keeps the tty in `signal_struct` and `proc_clear_tty(group_leader)` drops it process-wide. Moving `ctty` to `ThreadGroup` is a tty-lane change (console/routing, tty_ioctl, openat `/dev/tty`, clone) and is NOT done here. Harness: `tests/session.rs` setsid cases (new session+group, EPERM re-entry, EPERM as pgrp leader, EPERM on a pre-existing group number, whole-thread-group move, ctty cleared); `cargo test -p sched` 282/282; both arches build + boot. |

Closing it is a kernel-wide cred-model lane, not a cred-syscall-local fix. `ns_capable_setid` likewise degrades to a global effective-set check because capabilities are not per-user-namespace yet. |

FIXED: the limit is now `NGROUPS_MAX` (65536), not 32 — the credential holds a heap `group_info` instead of an inline 32-entry array, and the DAC snapshot (`vfs::Cred`) shares that whole list so a user in >32 groups is no longer silently denied. The list is `groups_sort`ed ascending before install (membership is now the `groups_search` binary search). Error ORDER matches Linux exactly: `EPERM` from `may_setgroups` FIRST (even for an illegal size and an unreadable pointer), then `(unsigned)gidsetsize > NGROUPS_MAX` -> `EINVAL`, then `ENOMEM` from the allocation, then per-element `EFAULT`/`EINVAL` left to right; a failed call leaves the previous list intact. `may_setgroups` now also honours the `/proc/PID/setgroups` deny policy (CVE-2014-8989) via `user_namespace::setgroups_policy`, which was previously ignored. Non-zero pids now resolve through `registry::lookup_in_namespace` in the CALLER's pid namespace (was `lookup_by_vpid`, namespace-blind); a negative pid is ESRCH by construction instead of wrapping into a huge u32; `pid==0` reads the caller directly. | 128 | 64 | `rt_sigtimedwait` | `sys_rt_sigtimedwait` | sched | fd-table, sched, signals, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:97` | `PARTIAL` | `F729-signal-syscall-audit` | `kernel/signal.c:3755 do_sigtimedwait, kernel/signal.c:3814 SYSCALL_DEFINE4(rt_sigtimedwait), kernel/signal.c:collect_signal` | `sched::sigaltstack` (14) + `sched::tests::signals` (24) + `syscall::sigset` (4) + `hal::write_siginfo` (3); x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds; `make smoke-x86` + `make smoke-arm` both reach `basic.target` | Full `do_sigtimedwait` audit against `linux-master/kernel/signal.c:3755`. FIXED: (a) SIGKILL/SIGSTOP were waitable — Linux `sigdelsetmask` strips them, else a waiter swallows a fatal signal and becomes unkillable; (b) a zero timeout returned EINTR when an unrelated signal was pending — Linux `if (!sig && timeout)` never sleeps, so the answer is EAGAIN; (c) `uinfo` was probed at entry, turning a timeout into EFAULT — Linux copies siginfo only after a successful dequeue; (d) dequeued only the thread-private set, so `sigwaitinfo` in a worker thread for a process-directed signal hung forever — now `sigpend::dequeue_signal` claims from thread-private then process-directed, atomically (one consumer wins, never a duplicate delivery); (e) a bitmap-only signal reported si_code 0 with no explicit fallback — now Linux `collect_signal`s SI_USER; (f) si_value was written but standard signals had no queue to hold it. Timespec validation + `ktime_set` clamp already correct via `syscall::time::timespec_to_ns`. The pre-existing park-before-recheck ordering (no lost wakeup) is preserved and re-verified. REMAINING: the "a signal OUTSIDE the set interrupts me" EINTR check reads only the thread-private deliverable set, so a process-directed out-of-set signal does not interrupt a non-leader waiter. The success path (waiting for and consuming a signal IN the set) does cover both sets. Closing this needs a real `signal_struct::shared_pending` that the DELIVERY path (`take_lowest_pending`) also dequeues from — a whole-signal-subsystem change, not a slot-128 one |

FIXED SECURITY: the si_code forgery guard ran only for signals 33..64 and only AFTER the ESRCH/EPERM checks, and compared internal tids; signals 1..32 were routed to `sys_kill` with the siginfo DISCARDED, so `rt_sigqueueinfo(other_pid, SIGUSR1, {si_code=SI_KERNEL})` returned 0 where Linux returns EPERM. Now `signum::sigqueueinfo_forgery_rejected` implements `(si_code >= 0 OR si_code == SI_TKILL) && task_pid_vnr(current) != pid` verbatim, against the pid ARGUMENT, before any other check, for every signal number. ALSO FIXED: siginfo copy-in was 32 bytes (Linux copies `sizeof(kernel_siginfo)` = 48); si_signo is now forced to `sig`; EINVAL/ESRCH/EPERM now follow `kill_proc_info` order; standard signals keep their queued siginfo — `Task::sigqueue` covers all 64 signals with Linux `legacy_queue` depth (1 record for standard, `RT_QUEUE_CAP` for RT), so `sigqueue(3)` on SIGUSR1 delivers its `sival_ptr` and glibc SIGCANCEL/SIGSETXID handlers see si_code. si_value is now written as a full 8-byte `_rt` `sigval_t` instead of a truncated 4-byte si_status. 297 `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` fixed in the same shape (EINVAL for non-positive tgid/tid; ESRCH unless tid is in tgid) |
| 130 | common | `rt_sigsuspend` | `sys_rt_sigsuspend` | sched | fd-table, sched, signals, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:96` | `PARTIAL` | `F729-signal-syscall-audit` | `kernel/signal.c:4843 sigsuspend, kernel/signal.c:4862 SYSCALL_DEFINE2(rt_sigsuspend), kernel/signal.c:signal_delivered, include/linux/sched/signal.h:sigmask_to_save` | `sched::sigaltstack` (14) + `sched::tests::signals` (24) + `syscall::sigset` (4) + `hal::write_siginfo` (3); x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds; `make smoke-x86` + `make smoke-arm` both reach `basic.target` | Full `sigsuspend` audit against `linux-master/kernel/signal.c:4843`. FIXED: the old mask was RESTORED before returning EINTR, so a handler dispatched on the way out ran under the CALLER's mask instead of the suspend mask — the exact race `sigsuspend(2)` exists to close. Now Linux `saved_sigmask` + `TIF_RESTORE_SIGMASK`: `Task::arm_saved_sigmask` installs the temporary mask and arms the restore; `Task::sigmask_to_save` (Linux `sigmask_to_save`) hands the signal frame the PRE-suspend mask so `rt_sigreturn` lands on it, while the handler runs under the suspend mask OR `sa_mask` OR the signal; `Task::restore_saved_sigmask` (Linux `restore_saved_sigmask`) puts it back at the syscall-return tail when no handler runs. The flag is one-shot, so exactly one of the two paths consumes it. ALSO FIXED: the wait was a `sti; pause; cli` + `tick_yield` busy-spin burning a CPU for the whole suspension — now a real `WaitList` park with publish-before-recheck (Linux `TASK_INTERRUPTIBLE; schedule()`). SIGKILL/SIGSTOP can never be blocked by the suspend mask. Always returns EINTR. NOTE: the wake condition is thread-private `deliverable_signals_self()`, matching what the delivery path can actually deliver; see the remaining-gap note below. REMAINING: the wake condition is thread-private `deliverable_signals_self()`, so a non-leader thread suspended for a PROCESS-directed signal does not wake. Deliberate, not an oversight: this kernel posts process-directed signals on the group leader and `take_lowest_pending` dequeues only the thread-private set, so waking on the shared set would return EINTR with no handler to run and spin the caller's `while (!flag) sigsuspend()` loop forever. Closing it needs the delivery path to dequeue process-directed signals too — a whole-signal-subsystem change, not a slot-130 one |
| 131 | 64 | `sigaltstack` | `sys_sigaltstack` | sched | fd-table, sched, signals, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:75` | `IMPL` | `F729-signal-syscall-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/signal.c, fs/signalfd.c` | `sched::sigaltstack` (14) + `sched::tests::signals` (24) + `syscall::sigset` (4) + `hal::write_siginfo` (3); x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds; `make smoke-x86` + `make smoke-arm` both reach `basic.target` | Full `do_sigaltstack` audit against `linux-master/kernel/signal.c:4398`. The old implementation recorded the stack and NOTHING ELSE — no EPERM, no EINVAL, no ENOMEM, no SS_DISABLE handling, `oss` written BEFORE `ss` was validated, and the alternate stack was never actually USED by signal delivery: a recorded-but-ignored sigaltstack that breaks every SIGSEGV crash handler. FIXED, all of it, via the new hosted-tested `sched::sigaltstack` policy module: EPERM when changing the stack while executing on it, EINVAL for an `ss_flags` mode outside {0, SS_ONSTACK, SS_DISABLE}, ENOMEM for `ss_size < MINSIGSTKSZ` (2048 x86_64 / 5120 aarch64), SS_DISABLE zeroing sp/size while keeping the flag bits, the Linux unchanged-request short circuit before the size check, `oss.ss_flags` computed by `sas_ss_flags()` (live mode + stored SS_AUTODISARM) rather than the stored word, and Linux's exact ordering (read `ss` -> validate/apply -> write `oss` last). DELIVERY now honours it: `fs::sig_dispatch::setup_altstack` applies Linux `sigsp()` and both `build_signal_frame`s place the rt_sigframe at the alt-stack top under SA_ONSTACK; `uc_stack` is populated (Linux `save_altstack_ex`) and `rt_sigreturn` re-arms it (Linux `restore_altstack`), so SS_AUTODISARM disarms for the handler and comes back after. Scope note: the SA_ONSTACK delivery path is verified by the hosted `sigsp`/`sas_ss_flags`/`apply` decision table plus both-arch build and boot to `basic.target`; it is NOT verified by an end-to-end guest program that faults onto its alternate stack |

Verified `struct statfs` is 120 B with the identical generic LP64 layout on x86_64 and aarch64 (so `sizeof(statfs)==sizeof(kstatfs)` and Linux takes the straight-memcpy path, no EOVERFLOW), and that resolution is a single `LOOKUP_FOLLOW` walk to the authoritative (vfsmount,dentry). FIXED divergences: (1) `calculate_f_flags` ALWAYS sets `ST_VALID` (0x0020) -- glibc's `statvfs` uses it to decide whether `f_flags` is meaningful; it was never set. `ST_NOSYMFOLLOW` (0x2000) mapping was also missing. (2) `fill_usage` FABRICATED block accounting -- `f_blocks=8192/f_bfree=4096` for anything ext4-magic and `f_blocks=1/f_bfree=0` for every pseudo-fs, explicitly so `df` would keep the row. Deleted: zero accounting is Linux's honest answer for a pseudo filesystem. (3) `f_namelen` was hardcoded 255 at the encoder and `f_frsize` aliased to `f_bsize`; both are now real `SbStatFs` fields, backend-owned, with `statfs_by_dentry`'s two defaults (NAME_MAX, `f_frsize<-f_bsize`) applied in `SuperBlock::statfs`. (4) per-fs `f_type`: `ramfs` reported TMPFS_MAGIC and the name `tmpfs` (it now has its own ctor, RAMFS_MAGIC 0x858458f6, and Linux ramfs's limit-less zero accounting); `fusectl` reported FUSE_SUPER_MAGIC 0x65735546 instead of FUSE_CTL_SUPER_MAGIC 0x65735543; nine `FsType` registry entries carried magic 0. (5) ext4 `f_blocks` used `blocks_count_lo` only (truncating a >16 TiB fs), `f_bavail` did not subtract `s_r_blocks_count` (overstating space to unprivileged writers), `f_namelen` was not EXT4_NAME_LEN and `f_fsid` was `s_dev` instead of Linux `uuid_to_fsid(s_uuid)`. Also fixed the ext4 superblock 64-bit high-half offsets, which were SWAPPED (`s_blocks_count_hi` read from 0x158 and `s_free_blocks_count_hi` read/WRITTEN at 0x150; the real order is 0x150/0x154/0x158) -- latent on a small fs, fs-corrupting on the counter-writeback path of a 64bit fs. Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --lib statfs_abi` (9 tests: 120-byte size, all uapi offsets, per-field placement, zeroed `f_spare[4]`, pseudo-fs zeros reported verbatim, MNT_*/SB_* -> ST_* by-name mapping, flag-set union, ST_* bit values); `cargo test -p fs --lib statfs_tests` (5, incl. ramfs/tmpfs distinct magic+name and unbounded-instance zero accounting); `cargo test -p ext4 --lib superblock` (20, incl. 64-bit offset order, per-counter high-half merge, 32-bit ignore, `uuid_to_fsid`); `cargo test -p vfs --test superblock_statfs_passthrough`. Both arches build. NOT verified: no live `df`/QEMU boot run in this lane. |

NOT matched: Linux's PRIO_USER loop also requires `task_pid_vnr(p)` (pid-namespace visibility); `security_task_setnice` has no LSM analogue here. Genuinely honoured: NORMAL (CFS weight), FIFO (RT priority buckets, preempts CFS), IDLE (weight only). Harness: `setscheduler_round_trips_every_implemented_policy`, `invalid_priority_is_einval_even_when_the_caller_would_be_denied`, `reset_on_fork_bit_is_recorded_and_reported`, `deadline_is_rejected_never_silently_run_as_normal`. |

| 155 | common | `pivot_root` | `sys_pivot_root` | vfs | dcache, fs-context, mntns, permissions, procfs, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:29; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/155_pivot_root.rs; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/pivot_root_policy.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/src/mount/pivot_check.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/src/mount/namespace.rs` | `PARTIAL` | `F739-pivot-root-adjtimex` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/namespace.c, fs/fs_struct.c, fs/internal.h` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | F739 rebuilt slot 155 against /home/nd/oxide/linux-master fs/namespace.c `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(pivot_root)` + `path_pivot_root()`. FIXED, all four previously-collapsed behaviours: (1) both lookups now report their real errno -- `Err(_) => EINVAL` swallowed ENOENT/ENOTDIR/ELOOP/EACCES/ENAMETOOLONG, so a caller naming a missing directory was told its arguments were malformed; (2) `LOOKUP_DIRECTORY` is enforced, so a non-directory argument is ENOTDIR; (3) CAP_SYS_ADMIN moved AFTER both lookups, matching Linux's split between the syscall wrapper and `path_pivot_root`, so a bad path no longer reports EPERM; and it is now `may_mount()` proper -- `ns_capable(mnt_ns->user_ns, CAP_SYS_ADMIN)` via `nscg::proc_ns::has_cap_for` rather than a flat effective-set test; (4) the previously-absent rungs are implemented -- `check_mnt(root_mnt)`/`check_mnt(new_mnt)` EINVAL, `d_unlinked(new->dentry)` ENOENT, `new_mnt == root_mnt` or `old_mnt == root_mnt` EBUSY, `path_mounted(&root)` EINVAL (a task chrooted into a plain directory), `path_mounted(new)` EINVAL -- in Linux's exact order, extracted into `vfs::mount::pivot_check` so the order is a hosted unit test. The caller's root now comes from its own `fs_struct` (`get_fs_root`), not an assumed namespace root. The dead `NR_PIVOT_ROOT` blanket-EPERM arm in `sched/src/compat.rs` (shadowed by route_c since F110) is removed. Two Linux lines are structurally inapplicable and documented as such rather than skipped silently: `!mnt_has_parent(new_mnt)` is implied by the EBUSY rung in a tree whose only self-parented mount is the namespace root, and `!mnt_has_parent(root_mnt)` guards Linux's `attach_mnt` into the old root's slot under `rootfs`, which this tree does not have. PARTIAL for one disclosed divergence: when the caller has chrooted into a DIFFERENT mount, Linux relocates that mount and leaves the namespace root alone, whereas `commit_retree` re-roots the whole namespace; the common container/systemd path (`chdir(new); pivot_root(".", ".")`, no chroot) is unaffected. Harness: `cargo test -p vfs` 1281 (11 new `mount::pivot_check` order tests + the pre-existing `mount_pivot_validation`, `mount_propagation_pivot`, `mount_root_truth` suites still green, including the systemd-shaped `full_service_setup_pivot_and_switch_root_detach`); `cargo test -p syscalls` 357 in the lib target (7 new `pivot_root_policy` sequence tests). Both arches build. |
