Built from /home/nd/oxide/linux-master: `kernel/time/time.c SYSCALL_DEFINE1(adjtimex)`, `kernel/time/timekeeping.c do_adjtimex`/`__do_adjtimex`/`timekeeping_validate_timex`/`__timekeeping_inject_offset`, and `kernel/time/ntp.c ntp_adjtimex`/`process_adjtimex_modes`/`process_adj_status`/`ntp_update_offset`/`ntp_update_offset_fll`/`ntp_update_frequency`/`second_overflow`/`ntp_offset_chunk`. `timekeeper::ntp::NtpState` is Linux's `struct ntp_data` field for field (tick_usec, tick_length, tick_length_base, time_state, time_status, time_offset, time_constant, time_maxerror, time_esterror, time_freq, time_reftime, time_adjust, ntp_next_leap_sec) with CONFIG_NTP_PPS off, matching ntp.c's `#else` arms exactly: `ntp_offset_chunk` is the plain `shift_right(offset, SHIFT_PLL + time_constant)`, no PPS status bit is ever raised, and `pps_fill_timex` reports zeros. Every mode is applied, not accepted-and-ignored: ADJ_STATUS (with STA_RONLY masking and the STA_PLL on/off edges), ADJ_NANO/ADJ_MICRO, ADJ_FREQUENCY (PPM_SCALE, clamped to MAXFREQ_SCALED), ADJ_MAXERROR/ADJ_ESTERROR (clamped to NTP_PHASE_LIMIT), ADJ_TIMECONST (clamp + the non-nanosecond +4), ADJ_TAI (0..=MAX_TAI_OFFSET, committed through the existing `timekeeper::set_tai_offset`, which had no caller until now), ADJ_OFFSET (the real PLL/FLL discipline), ADJ_TICK, ADJ_SETOFFSET (a real signed wall-clock step via `ClockState::inject_offset`, paired with `sched::timers::clock_was_set()` so absolute deadlines reproject), and the legacy `ADJ_ADJTIME` single-shot channel including its read-only `ADJ_OFFSET_SS_READ` form. The discipline actually STEERS the clock: `NtpState::advance()` runs `second_overflow` for each elapsed wall second (leap-second state machine, dispersion growth, phase draining, MAX_TICKADJ-capped adjtime slew) and integrates `tick_length` against elapsed MONOTONIC time -- not tick count, because this kernel programs a one-shot timer and its ticks are not evenly spaced -- applying the result through `ClockState::slew`, which deliberately does not bump the step generation because a slew must not invalidate absolute deadlines. Wired at the kmain tick hook ahead of the vvar publish; an undisciplined system costs one relaxed atomic load. Return value is the `TIME_*` state (TIME_OK/INS/DEL/OOP/WAIT/ERROR) including the leap-second rewrite of `time` and `tai`, matching the contract `crates/user/glibc/src/time/adjtime.rs` already documented. The 208-byte `struct __kernel_timex` codec is pinned against the offsets glibc asserts (offset 8, freq 16, status 40, constant 48, time 72, tick 88, tai 160, size 208) and zeroes both the three pad words and the eleven reserved tail words so no kernel stack residue leaks. Copy-back is unconditional, as in time.c. Harness: `cargo test -p timekeeper` 46 (was 3) covering the validation ladder, every mode, leap seconds, and the slew rate; `cargo test -p syscalls` timex_policy 16 + timex_abi 6. Both arches build. |
| 160 | common | `setrlimit` | `sys_setrlimit` | syscall-abi | sched, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/160_setrlimit.rs; crates/kernel/sched/src/task/rlimits.rs; crates/kernel/sched/src/rlimit.rs` | `PARTIAL` | `F742-system-control-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE2(setrlimit)+do_prlimit, fs/file.c:sysctl_nr_open` | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/rlimit_policy.rs` | F742 rebuilt slot 160 against /home/nd/oxide/linux-master `kernel/sys.c`. Linux's `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(setrlimit)` is `copy_from_user` then `do_prlimit(current, resource, &new_rlim, NULL)`, and the whole ladder now lives in ONE work-fn, `sched::Task::do_prlimit`, that slots 097/160/302 share (the resource-index test, `cur <= max`, the `fs.nr_open` ceiling and the hard-limit-raise test previously existed in three partial copies, or nowhere). FIXED, four divergences: (1) the ladder ran in the WRONG ORDER -- `resource >= RLIM_NLIMITS` was tested BEFORE the copy-in, so `setrlimit(9999, <unreadable>)` reported EINVAL where Linux reports EFAULT; the copy is now first, exactly as the syscall wrapper has it. (2) Raising the HARD limit needed no capability whatsoever: any process could hand itself `RLIMIT_NOFILE = RLIM_INFINITY`. It is now Linux's `capable(CAP_SYS_RESOURCE)` -- deliberately the INIT user namespace test rather than `ns_capable`, per the upstream comment 'Keep the capable check against init_user_ns until cgroups can contain all limits'. Lowering the hard limit stays unprivileged and irreversible. (3) `RLIMIT_NOFILE` above `fs.nr_open` was accepted; it is now EPERM, and `sysctl_nr_open` became a LIVE variable in its Linux owner (`fs/file.c` -> `vfs::fdtable::limits`) with `/proc/sys/fs/nr_open` repointed at it, so the ceiling the syscall enforces and the file userspace reads cannot disagree -- the leaf was previously a procfs-owned cell nothing consulted. (4) The read-decide-write is now one critical section under the thread group's rlimit lock, so two threads racing a raise cannot both observe the old hard limit and both raise it. Slot 302 was rerouted through the same work-fn in the same pass (a second copy of these rules would be exactly the split source of truth `CLAUDE.md` forbids), which also fixed `prlimit64` writing `old_rlim` BEFORE validating the new value -- Linux copies it out only on success. Rlimits already live on the shared `ThreadGroup` (Linux `signal->rlim`, F727); re-verified. PARTIAL is about ENFORCEMENT, not the syscall: only FSIZE, DATA, NOFILE, NICE, RTPRIO and STACK-at-execve are consulted by any code path. CPU, CORE, NPROC, MEMLOCK, AS, SIGPENDING, MSGQUEUE and RTTIME are stored and read back faithfully but nothing tests them, so `setrlimit` on those eight yields a durable value rather than a limit; each belongs to the lane that owns its enforcement point (fork, mmap, mlock, coredump, POSIX-CPU-timers, mqueue, RT throttle). RSS and LOCKS are inert upstream too. Harness: `cargo test -p syscalls` (8 new `rlimit_policy` tests driving the real `do_prlimit` over a real `sched::Task` -- ordering, the nr_open ceiling at and above the limit, privileged and unprivileged raise, irreversible lower, no-write-on-reject) plus 2 new `vfs::fdtable::limits` window tests. Both arches build; x86 and arm boot smoke reach `basic.target`. |
| 161 | common | `chroot` | `sys_chroot` | vfs | dcache, fs-context, permissions, security, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/161_chroot.rs; crates/kernel/fs/src/cwd.rs` | `IMPL` | `F742-system-control-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/open.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE1(chroot), fs/fs_struct.c:set_fs_root, fs/namei.c:path_permission` | `crates/kernel/fs/src/cwd.rs` | F742 rebuilt slot 161 against /home/nd/oxide/linux-master `fs/open.c`. Linux's order is `filename_lookup(LOOKUP_FOLLOW|LOOKUP_DIRECTORY)` -> `path_permission(MAY_EXEC|MAY_CHDIR)` -> `ns_capable(current_user_ns(), CAP_SYS_CHROOT)` -> `set_fs_root(current->fs, &path)`, and every rung is now present in that order in `fs::cwd::set_fs_root`, sitting beside `set_fs_pwd` in the same Linux-shaped owner. FIXED, three divergences: (1) the capability test ran FIRST, before the path was looked up, so an unprivileged caller naming a missing or non-directory path was told EPERM where Linux says ENOENT/ENOTDIR -- the wrong errno for every `RootDirectory=` failure; the lookup's own errno is now reported verbatim. (2) The `MAY_EXEC` search-permission test on the new root was skipped entirely, so a caller could chroot into a directory it had no right to traverse; a non-searchable target is now EACCES, decided before the capability test exactly as Linux orders it. (3) The flat effective-set test became `ns_capable(current_user_ns(), CAP_SYS_CHROOT)` via `nscg::proc_ns::has_cap_for`, scoping the capability to the caller's user namespace instead of reading a global bitmask. Verified unchanged-and-correct: the final symlink IS followed (`resolve_path_raw(.., no_follow_final=false)` = `LOOKUP_FOLLOW`); a non-directory target is ENOTDIR (`LOOKUP_DIRECTORY`); the new root is installed on the SHARED `FsContext` (oxide's `fs_struct`), so a `CLONE_FS` sibling observes it, a `fork(2)` child snapshots it and `unshare(CLONE_FS)` splits it; and the pwd is deliberately left alone, reproducing the `chroot`-without-`chdir` escape route Linux also leaves open. `getcwd(2)` already renders a pwd outside the root with Linux's `(unreachable)` prefix. Harness: the ladder is one work-fn, so its order is a single readable sequence rather than three call sites; `cargo test -p fs` and `cargo test -p syscalls` green. Both arches build; x86 and arm boot smoke reach `basic.target`. |

| 170 | common | `sethostname` | `sys_sethostname` | security | procfs, security, syscall-abi, sysfs, system-control, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/170_sethostname.rs; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/uts_policy.rs; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/171_setdomainname.rs` | `IMPL` | `F742-system-control-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE2(sethostname), include/uapi/linux/utsname.h:__NEW_UTS_LEN` | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/uts_policy.rs` | F742 rebuilt slots 170/171 against /home/nd/oxide/linux-master `kernel/sys.c`. Linux implements both as one routine over two `new_utsname` fields, so they now share one work-fn (`hostname::write_uts_name`) whose decision order is `uts_policy::check_uts_set`. FIXED, four divergences: (1) ORDER -- the length window was tested BEFORE the capability, so an unprivileged caller passing a bogus length got EINVAL where Linux gives EPERM, and learned whether its length was valid; `ns_capable` is first now, as in the source. (2) The capability was a flat effective-set test; it is now `ns_capable(current->nsproxy->uts_ns->user_ns, CAP_SYS_ADMIN)` through `nscg::proc_ns::has_cap_for`, scoped to the user namespace that OWNS the UTS namespace being written. (3) `len` is declared `int`: only the low 32 bits are significant and a negative value is EINVAL. The raw 64-bit register was used, so `len = 0x1_0000_0020` -- a valid 32 -- was rejected; it truncates first now. (4) The syscall path ran the stored bytes through the `/proc/sys/kernel/hostname` newline trimmer, so `sethostname(<foo-newline>, 4)` silently stored `foo`. Linux's `memcpy(u->nodename, tmp, len)` filters nothing; the store is verbatim and the trim moved to the procfs write hook, where `proc_dostring` is what does it upstream. Readers were audited alongside the writers, per the PR#4013 regression shape: `uname(2)` substituted `(none)` for an EMPTY nodename while `/proc/sys/kernel/hostname` reported the empty string -- two answers for one piece of state -- and reports verbatim now, with the `(none)` default living in the init-namespace storage seed exactly as Linux keeps it in `init_uts_ns`. The absent-owner fallback on both the read and the write side is the INIT namespace's value (the global static), never an empty string. `__NEW_UTS_LEN` is one constant shared by the window test and the copy buffer. Harness: `cargo test -p syscalls` (4 new `uts_policy` tests -- EPERM precedes EINVAL for every length, negative length, the 0..=64 window, and the 32-bit truncation). Both arches build; x86 and arm boot smoke reach `basic.target`. |

| 188 | common | `setxattr` | `sys_setxattr` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/188_setxattr.rs` -> `xattr_common::sys_setxattr_path` -> `fs::xattr::{import_set,set_on}` | `IMPL` | `F733-xattr-audit` | `fs/xattr.c, include/uapi/linux/xattr.h, include/uapi/linux/limits.h, security/commoncap.c, fs/posix_acl.c, fs/ext4/xattr.c` | `fs-xattr-policy-model` | Plain form: the final symlink IS followed (LookupFlags follow). setxattr_copy order now matches Linux exactly: unknown flag bits EINVAL, then name ERANGE/EFAULT, then size>XATTR_SIZE_MAX E2BIG BEFORE the value is copied (was: copy-then-check, a 4 GiB kalloc), and the whole import runs BEFORE path resolution so a bad name outranks ENOENT. XATTR_CREATE=EEXIST / XATTR_REPLACE=ENODATA atomically in the store; CREATE|REPLACE together is no longer a wrong EINVAL. security.capability takes the v2/v3 header check (EINVAL) then CAP_SETFCAP (EPERM); other security.* take CAP_SYS_ADMIN. system.posix_acl_{access,default} route through the ACL path (blob validation, owner check, default-on-dir-only, posix_acl_update_mode i_mode rewrite + trivial-ACL drop). Success fires fsnotify_xattr (IN_ATTRIB). xattr_permission rebuilt from source: may_write_xattr (S_IMMUTABLE/S_APPEND EPERM), security.*/system.* left to the LSM+handler, trusted.* CAP_SYS_ADMIN (read denial = ENODATA), user.* limited to REG/DIR/SOCK with the sticky-dir owner rule and then real inode_permission DAC; xattr_resolve_name gives EOPNOTSUPP for unregistered namespaces and EINVAL for a bare prefix. Backends: tmpfs SimpleXattrs in memory, ext4 written through to the on-disk ibody/external block (`crates/kernel/ext4/tests/xattr_image.rs::syscall_layer_policy_writes_reach_disk_on_ext4` sets via the policy layer, remounts, reads back from disk). Harness: 15 cases in `crates/kernel/fs/src/xattr/tests.rs` + 2 in `crates/kernel/fs/tests/xattr_syscall_model.rs` (real tmpfs tree) + 4 in `crates/kernel/ext4/tests/xattr_image.rs` (real ext4 image), all green. NOT verified: idmapped-mount id translation (HAS_UNMAPPED_ID / ACL uid remap) — the xattr path only ever sees the identity idmap today; and no QEMU boot was run for this lane. |

It routed to `sys_kill` before, which is a DIFFERENT syscall: kill(2) is thread-group directed with si_code SI_USER and gives pid<=0 pgrp/broadcast meanings, so `tkill(0,sig)` fanned the signal at the caller's whole process group, `tkill(-1,sig)` broadcast it, and no SI_TKILL record was queued at all (glibc `__nptl_setxid_sighandler` and SIGCANCEL both reject a zeroed si_pid/si_code). Now matches `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(tkill)`: EINVAL for tid<=0, then `do_tkill(0, pid, sig)` shared with slot 234 in `tkill_common.rs` so the pair cannot drift. Lookup is `find_task_by_vpid`-equivalent in the CALLER's pid namespace; a missing tid is ESRCH even for a bogus signal, because Linux validates `sig` inside `check_kill_permission` AFTER the lookup. sig==0 is the existence/permission probe. si_code SI_TKILL, si_pid = sender `task_tgid_vnr`, si_uid = sender REAL uid (`current_uid()` is `cred->uid`). Hosted: 5 `tkill_common` admission tests; arm64 nr 130 -> slot 200 (and NOT slot 62) asserted in `syscall::arm_abi` tests. |

| 202 | common | `futex` | `sys_futex` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:67` | `PARTIAL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/futex/*.c` | `syscall-abi-model` | B1419 audit: WAIT/WAIT_BITSET/WAKE/WAKE_BITSET/REQUEUE/CMP_REQUEUE/WAKE_OP implemented (fixed: wake_op oparg/cmparg sign-extension, WAIT_BITSET/WAKE_BITSET bitset filtering + bitset==0 EINVAL, FUTEX_CLOCK_REALTIME cmd gate, signal/timeout wake misclassification). PI ops (LOCK_PI/UNLOCK_PI/TRYLOCK_PI/WAIT_REQUEUE_PI/CMP_REQUEUE_PI/LOCK_PI2) and obsolete FUTEX_FD honestly return -ENOSYS (was silent `0`) — no rt_mutex/priority-inheritance implementation exists. F743 fixes the interrupted return. `wait_loop` returned a bare `-EINTR`, which dropped BOTH restarts Linux performs. Linux `__futex_wait` ends an interrupted wait with `-ERESTARTSYS` (`kernel/futex/waitwake.c:738`), and `futex_wait()` then either returns it untouched when the call carried no timeout (`waitwake.c:753-754`) or arms `futex_wait_restart` with the ABSOLUTE deadline and returns `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK` (`waitwake.c:759-767`). The discriminator is the presence of a timeout, never whether it was absolute or relative: FUTEX_WAIT's relative timespec is already converted to an absolute CLOCK_MONOTONIC deadline at syscall entry (`kernel/futex/syscalls.c:184-185`), so both ops reach `futex_wait()` indistinguishable. That rule lives in the non-gated `ipc::futex_restart` so it is hosted-tested. The restart block carries uaddr, the full op word, val, bitset and the absolute deadline; slot 219 replays it through `dispatch_timed`, so a repeatedly interrupted wait runs out its REMAINING timeout. `futex_waitv`/futex2 take the plain `-ERESTARTSYS` arm with no block, matching `futex_wait_multiple` (`waitwake.c:595-596`) and `syscalls.c:426`, which call `__futex_wait` directly. Hosted: `ipc::futex_core_hosted` proves the untimed wait returns ERESTARTSYS and arms nothing, and that a timed wait arms RESTART_FUTEX with the unmodified absolute deadline. PI ops remain honestly -ENOSYS as before. |
| 203 | common | `sched_setaffinity` | `sys_sched_setaffinity` | sched | fd-table, namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/203_sched_setaffinity.rs:24` | `PARTIAL` | `F736-affinity-getdents-audit` | `kernel/sched/syscalls.c, include/linux/cpumask.h, kernel/sched/core.c, kernel/cgroup/cpuset.c` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | Rewritten against `kernel/sched/syscalls.c` `sys_sched_setaffinity`/`sched_setaffinity`/`__sched_setaffinity`. `len` is BYTES with NO minimum on the SET side (Linux `get_user_cpu_mask` zero-fills then copies min(len, cpumask_size)); the old code rejected len<8 with EINVAL, breaking any narrower cpu_set_t. Errno order is Linux's: EFAULT (copy_from_user) -> ESRCH (find_task_by_vpid, resolved in the CALLER's pid namespace via registry::resolve_user_pid, a TID not a TGID) -> EINVAL (PF_NO_SETAFFINITY) -> EPERM (check_same_owner || CAP_SYS_NICE) -> EINVAL (mask naming no active CPU). The EPERM check was entirely missing before, so any task could repin any other task including root's. The stored mask is the request narrowed by the cpuset only, never by cpu_active_mask, so an offline CPU stays in the mask; the raw request is kept in `user_cpus_allowed` (Linux `user_cpus_ptr`) and `cpuset_cpus_allowed` holds the cgroup restriction, so `cpuset.cpus` and sched_setaffinity compose (`sched::affinity::compose`) instead of overwriting each other. `cpu_active_mask` now reads the authoritative online BITMAP rather than a count that assumed dense CPU ids. PF_NO_SETAFFINITY is set on the per-CPU kthreads (ksoftirqd/N, kworker/N) that kthread_bind pins. ENFORCEMENT: the mask is consulted by select_task_rq (wakeup placement), balance_once/newidle_balance (both migration directions), and now by initial placement -- wake_up_new_task and the SIGCONT re-enqueue routed through the raw local runqueue, ignoring the mask; both now go through place_runnable/select_task_rq. The mask is also inherited across fork/clone (Linux dup_task_struct), which it was not: a CPUAffinity= unit or taskset shell lost the mask on its next fork. PARTIAL: a task that is CURRENTLY RUNNING on a now-disallowed CPU is only nudged with need_resched + a reschedule IPI rather than synchronously migrated (Linux's stop-machine migration handshake), so it can keep running there until it next schedules. Tests: syscalls::affinity_abi (14 hosted), sched::tests::affinity + sched::affinity (7 hosted). |
| 204 | common | `sched_getaffinity` | `sys_sched_getaffinity` | sched | fd-table, namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/204_sched_getaffinity.rs:22` | `IMPL` | `F736-affinity-getdents-audit` | `kernel/sched/syscalls.c, include/linux/cpumask.h, kernel/sched/core.c, kernel/cgroup/cpuset.c` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | Rewritten against `kernel/sched/syscalls.c` `sys_sched_getaffinity`/`sched_getaffinity`. Entry checks in Linux order: `(len * BITS_PER_BYTE) < nr_cpu_ids` -> EINVAL, then `len & (sizeof(unsigned long)-1)` -> EINVAL; then the task lookup (ESRCH, resolved in the caller's pid namespace, pid 0 = the calling THREAD), then the copy-out (EFAULT) -- previously the buffer was validated before the lookup, so a bad pointer masked ESRCH. Returns the number of BYTES written, `min(len, cpumask_size())`, which is what glibc's wrapper uses to zero-fill `cpuset[ret..cpusetsize]` and what `__get_nprocs` loops on. Reported mask is `p->cpus_mask & cpu_active_mask`, read from the authoritative online bitmap. Tests: syscalls::affinity_abi (14 hosted) covers both EINVAL rules, the byte return value at len 8/16/128/1024, little-endian CPU bit order, and the active-mask narrowing. |

| 217 | common | `getdents64` | `sys_getdents64` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/217_getdents64.rs:57` | `PARTIAL` | `F736-affinity-getdents-audit` | `fs/readdir.c, include/linux/dirent.h, include/linux/fs.h dir_emit_dots, fs/ext4/dir.c` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | Rewritten against `fs/readdir.c` `SYSCALL_DEFINE3(getdents64)`/`filldir64`. `linux_dirent64` layout byte-exact (d_ino@0, d_off@8, d_reclen@16, d_type@18, d_name@19) and distinct from the legacy `linux_dirent`; reclen = ALIGN(offsetof(d_name)+namlen+1, sizeof(u64)). Return rule is Linux's tail: bytes already packed win over a late backend error, EINVAL only when the buffer cannot hold the FIRST record, 0 only at end-of-directory; `iterate_dir` stores f_pos error-or-not; last record's d_off sealed with the final ctx.pos; count truncated to unsigned int; ENOTDIR decided before any user access; `verify_dirent_name` EIO on empty/`/`/>=PATH_MAX names; signal_pending abandons the walk after the first record. `.`/`..` now emitted for EVERY filesystem (`vfs::readdir_dots`, cursors 0/1, child cookies shifted by 2) with an opt-out for ext4/FUSE which carry their own; before this they were missing on tmpfs, kernfs, devfs, devpts, procfs, sysfs, cgroupfs, tracefs, binfmt_misc and autofs. d_type is honest per backend: tmpfs/kernfs/procfs/sysfs/cgroupfs report the real per-inode type; ext4 decodes the on-disk file_type byte but only when EXT4_FEATURE_INCOMPAT_FILETYPE is set (otherwise DT_UNKNOWN, previously DT_REG for every entry including directories); FUSE passes the daemon's DT_* through including DT_UNKNOWN. ext4 readdir cookies are now byte offsets (Linux `ext4_readdir`) so lseek(d_off) is stable and one listing is O(N) not O(N^2), and a block read error or corrupt rec_len surfaces EIO instead of looking like end-of-directory. PARTIAL: the synthetic filesystems still use ordinal cursors, so an insert/unlink between two getdents calls shifts the cookies of later entries (Linux uses cursor dentries / stable hashes); the f_version stale-cursor restart limits this to a restart rather than a torn listing. Tests: syscalls::getdents_abi (20 hosted), crates/kernel/fs/tests/getdents_dots_tmpfs.rs (6, real tmpfs), crates/kernel/ext4/tests/getdents_dtype_image.rs (5, real ext4 image). |

| 219 | common | `restart_syscall` | `sys_restart_syscall` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/219_restart_syscall.rs:38` | `IMPL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/signal.c:3185 (SYSCALL_DEFINE0(restart_syscall)), kernel/signal.c:3191 (do_no_restart_syscall), include/linux/restart_block.h:26-57, include/linux/thread_info.h:83-89 (set_restart_fn), arch/x86/kernel/signal.c (handle_signal, arch_do_signal_or_restart, get_nr_restart_syscall), arch/arm64/kernel/signal.c, kernel/time/hrtimer.c:2425-2462 (hrtimer_nanosleep, do_nanosleep, hrtimer_nanosleep_restart), fs/select.c:1042-1058 (do_restart_poll), kernel/futex/waitwake.c:741-785 (futex_wait, futex_wait_restart)` | `syscall-abi-model` | F741 replaced the `NR_RESTART_SYSCALL => Some(EINTR)` compat stub with Linux's real mechanism. `Task::restart_block` is `struct restart_block` with the indirect call docs/07 5 bans replaced by a kind discriminant plus payload; slot 219 dispatches it and falls back to `do_no_restart_syscall` (EINTR) for a stale or unarmed block, exactly as Linux does. The signal-delivery decision lives in one hosted-tested owner, `syscall::restart::signal_restart_action`, mirroring `handle_signal` (handler frame built: ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK and ERESTARTNOHAND -> EINTR, ERESTARTSYS -> restart only under SA_RESTART, ERESTARTNOINTR -> always restart) and `arch_do_signal_or_restart` (no handler frame -- SIG_DFL, SIG_IGN, or `get_signal()` returning nothing: every code restarts the same call except ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK, which rewrites the syscall-number register to this slot). Both arches carry the frame rewrite: x86 `regs->ax = get_nr_restart_syscall(regs); regs->ip -= 2`, arm64 the same against x8 (which `oxide_lower_sync_restore` reloads) and ELR_EL1. F743 completes the client set, so every Linux `restart_block` payload now has its counterpart here: `nanosleep` (`RESTART_NANOSLEEP`, the absolute expiry plus rmtp, shared by nanosleep(2) and the RELATIVE form of clock_nanosleep(2)), `poll` (`RESTART_POLL`, ufds/nfds/has_timeout/end_time, Linux `do_restart_poll`, self-re-arming) and `futex` (`RESTART_FUTEX`, uaddr/op/val/bitset plus the absolute deadline, Linux `futex_wait_restart`). The syscalls that Linux deliberately leaves blockless now say so with the right code instead of EINTR: ppoll/pselect6/select return `-ERESTARTNOHAND` and restart in place off the residual timeout they wrote back, `TIMER_ABSTIME` clock_nanosleep returns `-ERESTARTNOHAND`, and an untimed futex wait returns `-ERESTARTSYS`. F743 also repaired the syscall-return tail's job-control-stop arm, which returned the raw retval after `stop_until_cont_sig` -- it would have leaked the internal -512/-514/-516 sentinels to userspace as bogus errnos, and it skipped the restart Linux performs there (a stop builds no handler frame, so `arch_do_signal_or_restart` applies once SIGCONT resumes the task). Hosted: 7 `syscall::restart` decision tests, 4 `sched::task::restart` block tests, 5 slot-219 continuation-table tests, arm64 nr 128 round-trip, plus the per-client payload tests on rows 7, 35, 202 and 230. |

Fixed: the timespec decode used a bare saturating multiply and now goes through the shared `syscall::time::timespec_to_ns` (`ktime_set`) clamp, so a huge tv_sec can no longer install a deadline past KTIME_MAX; the timer-id lookup now rejects ids outside 0..INT_MAX the way `lock_timer` does. TIMER_ABSTIME takes the value verbatim (an already-past absolute deadline queues its signal at once), a relative value arms at now+value, a zero it_value disarms and drops the interval, and a relative CLOCK_REALTIME/CLOCK_TAI timer arms on CLOCK_MONOTONIC while an absolute one stays on the wall clock and is reprojected by `clock_was_set` — ALARM clocks keep their own base per `alarm_timer_arm`. Hosted: `sched::timers::tests` ABSTIME, past-deadline, disarm and rearm cases. |

Clock distinctness verified: REALTIME/COARSE/ALARM read the wall clock, MONOTONIC/RAW/COARSE the monotonic base, BOOTTIME/BOOTTIME_ALARM the boot base, TAI the wall clock plus the leap offset, and the CPU clocks task or process CPU time — none aliased to another. MONOTONIC_RAW equals MONOTONIC and BOOTTIME equals MONOTONIC by construction, which is accurate rather than a shortcut: the timekeeper applies no NTP frequency slew and no suspend has occurred. CLOCK_TAI is structurally distinct but its offset is always 0 because nothing sets it — `adjtimex` (159) and `clock_adjtime` (305) have no route, which is a gap in THOSE rows. CLOCKFD ids are EINVAL, matching `pc_clock_gettime` on a system with no `posix_clock` character device. EINVAL precedes EFAULT. Hosted: `syscalls::clock_policy::tests` (12) and `sched::posix_clock::tests` (5). |

| 230 | common | `clock_nanosleep` | `sys_clock_nanosleep` | time | fd-table, sched-clock, syscall-abi, time, timers, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:77` | `PARTIAL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/time/posix-timers.c, fs/timerfd.c, kernel/time/time.c` | `time-timerfd-model` | Ledger G2/B638: nanosleep and clock_nanosleep now check pending unmasked signals and return EINTR/rem semantics; remaining full-row audit covers absolute timers, restart behavior, and invalid timespec ordering. F743 closes the restart and signal-triage gaps. The slot no longer carries its own signal check: it used the RAW `sigpending & !sigmask`, so a signal Linux drops at SEND time (`kernel/signal.c:1051` `prepare_signal` -> `sig_ignored`; SIG_IGN, or SIG_DFL over SIG_KERNEL_IGNORE_MASK = SIGCHLD/SIGCONT/SIGWINCH/SIGURG) truncated the sleep -- a terminal resize cut every `clock_nanosleep` short. It now shares `nanosleep(2)`'s engine and `Task::sleep_wake`. The ABS/REL split is Linux's: `TIMER_ABSTIME` forces `rmtp = NULL` at the syscall entry (`kernel/time/posix-timers.c:1400-1401`), which makes `restart->nanosleep.type` TT_NONE so no remaining time is ever copied out, and `hrtimer_nanosleep` then returns `-ERESTARTNOHAND` and arms NO block (`kernel/time/hrtimer.c:2449-2453`); the relative form copies the remainder to `rmtp`, arms `hrtimer_nanosleep_restart` with the ABSOLUTE expiry, and returns `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK`. `restart_block.fn = do_no_restart_syscall` is set on entry per `posix-timers.c:1402`. The wait also parks on the scheduler wait list with an absolute deadline instead of busy-yielding. Clock admission already matched `k_clock.nsleep` presence (EINVAL for unknown, EOPNOTSUPP for CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID and the coarse/raw clocks, CAP_WAKE_ALARM for the alarm clocks). Next audit: the CPU-time clocks. Linux sleeps `CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID` against consumed CPU time via `do_cpu_nanosleep`/`posix_cpu_nsleep_restart` (`kernel/time/posix-cpu-timers.c:1630-1665`), while this slot converts every clock into a wall-clock monotonic deadline, so a process-CPU sleep expires on elapsed time rather than CPU time. F751 implements CPU-time sleeping, closing the gap this row named. `clock_nanosleep` on a CPU clock no longer converts to a wall deadline: it arms a timer ON THE CPU CLOCK in the thread group's slot table with a wake-instead-of-signal notification, mirroring Linux `do_cpu_nanosleep`'s temporary `k_itimer` with `it.cpu.nanosleep = true` (`posix-cpu-timers.c:1537-1626`) and `cpu_timer_fire`'s wake branch (`:682-688`). It must be event-driven because a sleeping task accrues no CPU time, so only a RUNNING sibling advances the clock — which is also why a per-thread clock naming the caller is rejected with EINVAL up front (`:1639-1642`). The ABS/REL split is Linux's (`:1646-1653`) and `RESTART_CPU_NANOSLEEP` carries the ABSOLUTE CPU expiry so a resumed sleep owes only its remainder, per `posix_cpu_nsleep_restart` (`:1657-1665`). Residual: a DYNAMIC per-thread clock naming pid 0 reports EOPNOTSUPP where Linux reports EINVAL — `classify_clock` maps it to the same `CpuEncoded` value as the static CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID, which Linux separates by k_clock table, so telling them apart needs static-vs-dynamic provenance on the clock spec. `CpuMeasure::Sched` is still `utime+stime` where Linux `CPUCLOCK_SCHED` is `task_sched_runtime()`. No guest differential yet. |

Exit code truncated `& 0xff` per `SYSCALL_DEFINE1(exit_group)`. Plain `exit(2)` verified to leave the group alive. Same `disassociate_ctty(1)` gap as row 60. Hosted: `sched::exit::tests::group`/`thread_group` prove the non-leader group-code path. |

| 264 | common | `renameat` | `sys_renameat` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/264_renameat.rs` | `PARTIAL` | `F740-rename-family-audit` | `fs/namei.c:filename_renameat2`, `fs/namei.c:__start_renaming`, `fs/namei.c:lock_two_directories`, `fs/namei.c:vfs_rename`, `fs/namei.c:may_delete_dentry`, `fs/namei.c:may_create_dentry`, `fs/namei.c:lookup_one_qstr_excl`, `fs/ext4/namei.c:ext4_rename2`, `fs/ext4/namei.c:ext4_rename`, `fs/ext4/namei.c:ext4_cross_rename`, `mm/shmem.c:shmem_rename2`, `fs/libfs.c:simple_rename_exchange`, `fs/kernfs/dir.c:kernfs_iop_rename` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | Rebuilt as `filename_renameat2` step for step, with the order-sensitive decisions in the ungated `syscalls/src/rename_policy.rs` so they are unit-testable: flags validated before either pathname is fetched (`RENAME_NOREPLACE|RENAME_WHITEOUT` legal, EXCHANGE conflicts with both); old parent resolved before the new pathname is read; EXDEV before the LAST_NORM tests; non-`LAST_NORM` old side EBUSY, new side EBUSY except EEXIST under NOREPLACE (Linux's `error = -EEXIST` assignment sits between the two tests); EROFS via the owning mount; source-missing ENOENT, EXCHANGE-needs-both ENOENT, NOREPLACE EEXIST; the `lock_rename` ancestor TRAP split — source-is-ancestor EINVAL, target-is-ancestor ENOTEMPTY (EINVAL under EXCHANGE) — which had no counterpart before; trailing-slash ENOTDIR; same-inode no-op 0 after those gates; mountpoint EBUSY via `d_mountpoint`; then Landlock and `may_rename`. Backends: ext4 gained the `ext4_empty_dir` ENOTEMPTY gate (a populated directory victim previously went straight to `Mount::rmdir`, whose contract is caller-verified-empty, freeing the destination subtree's blocks and inode with its children still linked), the cross-parent `..` repoint plus both parents' `i_links_count` fixups on plain rename AND on EXCHANGE's mixed pair, the `EXT4_LINK_MAX` EMLINK ceiling, and the four `ext4_rename` timestamp stamps; the three path-based ext4 entry points now share one core with `i_op->rename` instead of three divergent copies. tmpfs gained the same ENOTEMPTY gate, `simple_rename`/`simple_rename_exchange` parent-link accounting, and EINVAL for flags outside the three shmem implements. kernfs now answers ANY renameat2 flag with EINVAL (`kernfs_iop_rename`'s `if (flags) return -EINVAL`) instead of honouring NOREPLACE/EXCHANGE and dropping WHITEOUT. A filesystem with no `->rename` slot now answers EPERM, not EROFS. Remaining for IMPL: `IS_SWAPFILE` EPERM, `HAS_UNMAPPED_ID`/invalid-vfsuid EOVERFLOW and `IS_DEADDIR` ENOENT inside `may_delete`/`may_create`, and the ESTALE `LOOKUP_REVAL` retry loop. Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --test rename_ladder_oracle` (7 differential cases run the REAL `renameat2(2)` on the host kernel and require the oxide ladder to return the identical errno); `cargo test -p syscalls --lib rename_policy` (15); `cargo test -p ext4 --test rename_nonempty_victim_image --test rename_dotdot_image --test rename_overwrite_image --test rename_exchange_image` (14); `cargo test -p fs --lib tmpfs::tests::rename` (7); `cargo run -p xtask -- kernel --arch x86_64` and `--arch aarch64`. |

| 270 | common | `pselect6` | `sys_pselect6` | vfs | fd-table, fs-special, poll, signal, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs, waitqueue | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:45` | `IMPL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/select.c:780-798 get_sigset_argpack, fs/select.c:748-778 do_pselect, fs/select.c:894-916 SYSCALL_DEFINE5(ppoll), fs/select.c:283-350 poll_select_finish, fs/select.c:257-272 poll_select_set_timeout, fs/select.c:483-620 do_select, fs/select.c:621-696 core_sys_select, fs/select.c:700-745 do_poll, fs/select.c:838-885 do_sys_poll, kernel/signal.c:3282 set_user_sigmask, include/linux/sched/signal.h:541-555 restore_saved_sigmask_unless`; B854-socket-file-ownership | `fd-vfs-io-model; syscalls::pselect6_sigmask_hosted (12); syscalls::pselect_ppoll unit tests (11); fs::sys_select_shape; syscalls::select_file_ownership_hosted; x86_64 and aarch64 kernel builds` | Audited line-by-line against Linux `SYSCALL_DEFINE6(pselect6)` -> `get_sigset_argpack` -> `do_pselect`. The 6th argument is read as `struct sigset_argpack { const sigset_t *ss; size_t ss_len; }` FIRST, so a faulting pack is EFAULT even when the timespec is also malformed, and the installed mask comes from `*ss` rather than the pack word itself. The sigmask handshake is now Linux's, not a swap-and-swap-back: `set_user_sigmask` arms `Task::arm_saved_sigmask` (Linux `saved_sigmask = blocked; set_current_blocked(new); set_restore_sigmask()`) and `poll_select_finish` calls `restore_saved_sigmask_unless(ret == -ERESTARTNOHAND)`, so an interrupted wait keeps the temporary mask for the handler and `rt_sigreturn` lands on the caller's original mask via `Task::sigmask_to_save`; the earlier eager restore reopened the delivery race the syscall exists to close. A NULL sigset pointer leaves the mask untouched whatever `sigsetsize` says; a non-NULL one is size-checked through the shared `syscall::sigset::check_exact`. `poll_select_set_timeout` reads the `struct __kernel_timespec` (EFAULT), validates it through the shared `syscall::time::timespec_to_ns` `ktime_set` clamp (EINVAL for tv_sec < 0 or tv_nsec outside [0, 1e9)), and folds it into an absolute monotonic end_time before the mask is installed and before the wait. NULL timeout waits indefinitely; a {0,0} timeout makes exactly one non-blocking pass. `poll_select_finish` writes the remaining time back to the caller's timespec as the raw syscall does (glibc's wrappers hide it behind a local copy), skipping the update for a zero timeout and for a `STICKY_TIMEOUTS` persona, and swallowing a writeback fault exactly as Linux does. `crate::pselect_ppoll::wait_verdict` now owns `do_poll`/`do_select` break order for both engines, so readiness outranks a deliverable signal which outranks an expired deadline: a zero timeout with a pending signal is EINTR, matching `count = -ERESTARTNOHAND` before `if (count or timed_out) break`. `signal_pending` is `Task::deliverable_signals`, the same `sig_ignored`-filtered definition every other blocking path uses, so a default-ignored signal cannot spuriously interrupt an event loop. Register-then-recheck holds: the waiter subscribes to every polled fd before the first readiness scan, the notification generation is snapshot before the scan, and `PollWaiter::park_until` publishes Sleeping before comparing it, so a wakeup landing in the scan-to-park gap cannot be lost. Busy-polling (`POLL_BUSY_LOOP`) is a `SO_BUSY_POLL`/`net.core.busy_poll` latency path owned by the socket-option rows and is off in Linux's default configuration, where these engines take the identical path. An interrupted select leaves the caller's fd sets byte-for-byte untouched (`core_sys_select`'s `if (ret < 0) goto out`), negative `nfds` is EINVAL, and a set bit naming an unopened fd is EBADF as `max_select_fd` reports it. B854 file retention across close/reuse is preserved. F743: `core_sys_select` now surfaces Linux's `-ERESTARTNOHAND` to the syscall tail instead of a pre-folded `-EINTR`. That distinction is the whole point of the code: with a handler frame built, `handle_signal` turns ERESTARTNOHAND into EINTR (unchanged behaviour for userspace), but with NO handler frame -- SIG_DFL, SIG_IGN, a job-control stop, or a raced dequeue -- `arch_do_signal_or_restart` RESTARTS the call, which this kernel previously never did. pselect6 arms no restart block, matching `do_pselect` (`fs/select.c:739-772`): the residual timeout written back to the caller's timespec is what makes an in-place restart correct. `poll_select_finish` now also implements the `sticky:` tail (`fs/select.c:361-363`), folding ERESTARTNOHAND to EINTR exactly when that writeback could not happen (STICKY_TIMEOUTS or a faulting copyout), per the comment at `fs/select.c:353` -- 'because we are not updating the timeval, we cannot restart the system call'. Rules owned by `pselect_ppoll::{wait_verdict, timeout_writeback_plan, finish_return}` with hosted tests. |
| 271 | common | `ppoll` | `sys_ppoll` | vfs | fd-table, fs-special, poll, rlimit, signal, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs, waitqueue | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:43` | `IMPL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/select.c:780-798 get_sigset_argpack, fs/select.c:748-778 do_pselect, fs/select.c:894-916 SYSCALL_DEFINE5(ppoll), fs/select.c:283-350 poll_select_finish, fs/select.c:257-272 poll_select_set_timeout, fs/select.c:483-620 do_select, fs/select.c:621-696 core_sys_select, fs/select.c:700-745 do_poll, fs/select.c:838-885 do_sys_poll, kernel/signal.c:3282 set_user_sigmask, include/linux/sched/signal.h:541-555 restore_saved_sigmask_unless`; B854-socket-file-ownership | `fd-vfs-io-model; syscalls::ppoll_sigmask_hosted (14); syscalls::pselect_ppoll unit tests (11); fs::sys_poll_shape; syscalls::poll_ownership_tests; x86_64 and aarch64 kernel builds` | Audited line-by-line against Linux `SYSCALL_DEFINE5(ppoll)` -> `do_sys_poll`. The sigmask handshake is now Linux's, not a swap-and-swap-back: `set_user_sigmask` arms `Task::arm_saved_sigmask` (Linux `saved_sigmask = blocked; set_current_blocked(new); set_restore_sigmask()`) and `poll_select_finish` calls `restore_saved_sigmask_unless(ret == -ERESTARTNOHAND)`, so an interrupted wait keeps the temporary mask for the handler and `rt_sigreturn` lands on the caller's original mask via `Task::sigmask_to_save`; the earlier eager restore reopened the delivery race the syscall exists to close. A NULL sigset pointer leaves the mask untouched whatever `sigsetsize` says; a non-NULL one is size-checked through the shared `syscall::sigset::check_exact`. `poll_select_set_timeout` reads the `struct __kernel_timespec` (EFAULT), validates it through the shared `syscall::time::timespec_to_ns` `ktime_set` clamp (EINVAL for tv_sec < 0 or tv_nsec outside [0, 1e9)), and folds it into an absolute monotonic end_time before the mask is installed and before the wait. NULL timeout waits indefinitely; a {0,0} timeout makes exactly one non-blocking pass. `poll_select_finish` writes the remaining time back to the caller's timespec as the raw syscall does (glibc's wrappers hide it behind a local copy), skipping the update for a zero timeout and for a `STICKY_TIMEOUTS` persona, and swallowing a writeback fault exactly as Linux does. `crate::pselect_ppoll::wait_verdict` now owns `do_poll`/`do_select` break order for both engines, so readiness outranks a deliverable signal which outranks an expired deadline: a zero timeout with a pending signal is EINTR, matching `count = -ERESTARTNOHAND` before `if (count or timed_out) break`. `signal_pending` is `Task::deliverable_signals`, the same `sig_ignored`-filtered definition every other blocking path uses, so a default-ignored signal cannot spuriously interrupt an event loop. Register-then-recheck holds: the waiter subscribes to every polled fd before the first readiness scan, the notification generation is snapshot before the scan, and `PollWaiter::park_until` publishes Sleeping before comparing it, so a wakeup landing in the scan-to-park gap cannot be lost. Busy-polling (`POLL_BUSY_LOOP`) is a `SO_BUSY_POLL`/`net.core.busy_poll` latency path owned by the socket-option rows and is off in Linux's default configuration, where these engines take the identical path. `nfds` above the caller's `RLIMIT_NOFILE` soft limit is EINVAL before any user copy, and `revents` are copied back unconditionally after `do_poll` returns, so an interrupted ppoll zeroes the caller's `revents` as Linux does instead of leaving the previous call's values. B854 file retention across close/reuse is preserved. F743: `do_sys_poll` now surfaces Linux's `-ERESTARTNOHAND` to the syscall tail instead of a pre-folded `-EINTR`, so the no-handler cases Linux restarts (SIG_DFL, SIG_IGN, job-control stop, raced dequeue) restart here too, while a delivered handler still reports EINTR exactly as before. ppoll arms no restart block, matching `SYSCALL_DEFINE5(ppoll)` (`fs/select.c:1092-1114`): the residual timeout written back to the caller's timespec is what makes the in-place restart correct, and only plain `poll(2)` needs a block. `poll_select_finish` now implements the `sticky:` fold (`fs/select.c:361-363`) so ERESTARTNOHAND becomes EINTR exactly when the writeback could not happen. Hosted: `syscalls::ppoll_sigmask_hosted` adds the interrupted-with-writeback and interrupted-under-STICKY_TIMEOUTS cases. |
| 272 | common | `unshare` | `sys_unshare` | sched | cgroup, fd-table, ipc, namespaces, procfs, sched, security, syscall-abi, task-lifetime | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/272_unshare.rs; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/unshare_policy.rs; crates/kernel/nscg/src/cgroup_ns.rs` | `PARTIAL` | `F742-system-control-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/fork.c:ksys_unshare+check_unshare_flags, kernel/nsproxy.c:unshare_nsproxy_namespaces, kernel/user_namespace.c:create_user_ns, kernel/cgroup/cgroup.c:copy_cgroup_ns` | `crates/kernel/syscalls/tests/namespace_ownership_hosted.rs` | F742 audited slot 272 against /home/nd/oxide/linux-master `kernel/fork.c` + `kernel/nsproxy.c`. FIXED, four divergences, one of them a security hole: (1) THERE WAS NO CAPABILITY TEST AT ALL. Linux gates the entire namespace set behind ONE `ns_capable(user_ns, CAP_SYS_ADMIN)` in `unshare_nsproxy_namespaces`; lacking it, any unprivileged process could hand itself a private mount, network, UTS, IPC, PID, cgroup or time namespace. The gate is in, scoped through `nscg::proc_ns::has_cap_for`, carrying Linux's exemption for `CLONE_NEWUSER` alone (`CLONE_NS_ALL & ~CLONE_NEWUSER`). (2) `check_unshare_flags`'s thread rungs were absent -- `CLONE_THREAD`/`CLONE_SIGHAND`/`CLONE_VM` were accepted and dropped on the floor. Linux does NOT reject them outright (this lane's briefing had that backwards): it accepts them as no-ops when there is nothing to unshare, and returns EINVAL when `!thread_group_empty(current)`, when `sighand->count > 1` for SIGHAND/VM, or when the caller is multi-threaded for VM. All three rungs are implemented, which is also what makes `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)` from a multi-threaded process EINVAL through its `CLONE_THREAD` implication. (3) `CLONE_SYSVSEM` and `CLONE_NEWIPC` were accepted without running Linux's `exit_sem(current)` -- 'CLONE_SYSVSEM is equivalent to sys_exit()' -- so a caller kept SEM_UNDO adjustments naming arrays it could no longer reach; the detach now runs once every replacement has succeeded. (4) `CLONE_NEWCGROUP` allocated a namespace identity nothing consulted, so a task inside it still read the host's absolute cgroup path. `nscg::cgroup_ns` now holds Linux's `copy_cgroup_ns` state -- the creating task's cgroup becomes the namespace root -- and `/proc/<pid>/cgroup` renders with `cgroup_path_ns_locked` semantics against the READER's namespace, including kernfs's `..`-relative form for a target outside the reader's root. Per-flag verification of what is genuinely created rather than recorded: NEWNS allocates a real `vfs::mntns` and copies the mount table plus the devfs view into it, remapping the caller's own fs paths; NEWUTS allocates real `nscg::uts_ns` storage seeded from the parent; NEWIPC keys real SysV sem/shm/msg and POSIX mqueue lookups; NEWNET creates a real `net::net_ns`; NEWPID is correctly for-children-only and leaves the caller's own pid namespace in place; NEWTIME clones real monotonic/boottime offsets into a for-children namespace; NEWUSER allocates a real parented user namespace that `has_cap_for` and the `/proc/<pid>/uid_map` machinery already scope against; NEWCGROUP is real as of this row. `CLONE_FILES` and `CLONE_FS` detach real private owners. PARTIAL for one disclosed gap: Linux's `create_user_ns` gives the new credentials a full capability set INSIDE the new user namespace, which is what lets a rootless caller stack `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER | CLONE_NEWNS)` in one call. Oxide's capability model is a single global effective mask with namespace ancestry applied at each call site, so granting that set here would make every un-scoped `has_cap()` call site in the tree answer true -- the grant needs per-user-namespace credential scoping in `sched::cred` (`docs/27`) first, and until then an unprivileged rootless `unshare` fails closed with EPERM rather than succeeding. Second disclosed gap: cgroupfs MOUNT rooting inside a cgroup namespace still exposes the whole tree; only the `/proc/<pid>/cgroup` rendering is namespace-relative. Harness: `cargo test -p syscalls --test namespace_ownership_hosted` (30 tests; 7 new end-to-end ones drive the real `sys_unshare` over a real `sched::Task` -- per-flag EPERM without CAP_SYS_ADMIN, NEWUSER-alone exempt, FILES/FS/SYSVSEM uncapped, single-threaded thread-flag no-ops, unknown-bit EINVAL with no mutation, SEM_UNDO detach through the real `ipc` undo list, cgroup-namespace root pinning) plus 10 new `unshare_policy` unit tests for the implied-flag expansion and the EINVAL ladder, and 12 new `nscg::cgroup_ns` tests. Both arches build; x86 and arm boot smoke reach `basic.target`. |

Built from /home/nd/oxide/linux-master `kernel/time/posix-timers.c SYSCALL_DEFINE2(clock_adjtime)` + `do_clock_adjtime()` + the `posix_clocks[]` `k_clock` table. It shares slot 159's discipline loop and differs in exactly the three ways Linux does, each covered by a test: the buffer is copied in BEFORE `which_clock` is examined, so a bad pointer is EFAULT even for a nonsense clock id; a valid `posix_clocks[]` entry with no `clock_adj` callback is EOPNOTSUPP, distinct from the EINVAL of an id outside the table -- collapsing the two would tell an NTP client its clock id was malformed when the clock is merely undisciplined; and the copy-back is conditional on success (`if (err >= 0 && copy_to_user(...))`) where 159's is unconditional. The admission table is `sched::posix_clock::adjustable`, added alongside the existing `settable`/`timer_creatable`/`nsleep_supported` callback-presence tables so all four state the same `k_clock` struct: only `clock_realtime` carries `.clock_adj` (`posix_clock_realtime_adj` -> `do_adjtimex`), and the CLOCKFD dynamic clocks are EINVAL until a `posix_clock` character device is registered, mirroring the existing `getres_ns` treatment. CLOCK_TAI is EOPNOTSUPP here on purpose -- `clock_tai` has no `.clock_adj` in Linux, and its offset is disciplined through CLOCK_REALTIME's `ADJ_TAI`. Harness: `cargo test -p syscalls` timex_policy 16, including the EOPNOTSUPP sweep over every non-realtime kclock and the copy-back asymmetry; `cargo test -p sched` clockid callback-presence table extended with `adjustable`. Both arches build. |

Backends: ext4 gained the `ext4_empty_dir` ENOTEMPTY gate (a populated directory victim previously went straight to `Mount::rmdir`, whose contract is caller-verified-empty, freeing the destination subtree's blocks and inode with its children still linked), the cross-parent `..` repoint plus both parents' `i_links_count` fixups on plain rename AND on EXCHANGE's mixed pair, the `EXT4_LINK_MAX` EMLINK ceiling, and the four `ext4_rename` timestamp stamps; the three path-based ext4 entry points now share one core with `i_op->rename` instead of three divergent copies. tmpfs gained the same ENOTEMPTY gate, `simple_rename`/`simple_rename_exchange` parent-link accounting, and EINVAL for flags outside the three shmem implements. kernfs now answers ANY renameat2 flag with EINVAL (`kernfs_iop_rename`'s `if (flags) return -EINVAL`) instead of honouring NOREPLACE/EXCHANGE and dropping WHITEOUT. A filesystem with no `->rename` slot now answers EPERM, not EROFS. Remaining for IMPL: `IS_SWAPFILE` EPERM, `HAS_UNMAPPED_ID`/invalid-vfsuid EOVERFLOW and `IS_DEADDIR` ENOENT inside `may_delete`/`may_create`, and the ESTALE `LOOKUP_REVAL` retry loop. Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --test rename_ladder_oracle` (7 differential cases run the REAL `renameat2(2)` on the host kernel and require the oxide ladder to return the identical errno); `cargo test -p syscalls --lib rename_policy` (15); `cargo test -p ext4 --test rename_nonempty_victim_image --test rename_dotdot_image --test rename_overwrite_image --test rename_exchange_image` (14); `cargo test -p fs --lib tmpfs::tests::rename` (7); `cargo run -p xtask -- kernel --arch x86_64` and `--arch aarch64`. |

Now real. IMPLEMENTED (advertised in `MEMBARRIER_CMD_QUERY` = 0x21f): `GLOBAL` (synchronize_rcu when >1 CPU online), `GLOBAL_EXPEDITED`, `REGISTER_GLOBAL_EXPEDITED`, `PRIVATE_EXPEDITED` (EPERM until registered), `REGISTER_PRIVATE_EXPEDITED`, `GET_REGISTRATIONS`. Expedited barriers = real cross-CPU IPI (resched vector / SGI) + ACK wait, both arches. REFUSED with EINVAL and deliberately absent from the QUERY mask (Linux does the same without `CONFIG_ARCH_HAS_MEMBARRIER_SYNC_CORE` / `CONFIG_RSEQ`): `PRIVATE_EXPEDITED_SYNC_CORE` + its REGISTER (no return-to-user core-serialization hook for descheduled threads), `PRIVATE_EXPEDITED_RSEQ` + its REGISTER (`sched::rseq` has no rseq_cs / IP-fixup, so no critical section could be restarted). PARTIAL until those four land. Admission logic + QUERY mask hosted-tested in `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/membarrier/tests.rs`; per-mm registration in `crates/kernel/mm-vmm/src/address_space/membarrier.rs`. |

STATUS CORRECTED IN-PROGRESS->PARTIAL (D394): the branch named in the Branch column is merged and deleted, so the work landed long ago. IN-PROGRESS means 'local uncommitted work is currently touching this syscall' -- leaving it set on merged work tells the next agent a live lane owns this row, which is exactly how duplicate lanes get opened. ## B865 Non-Network Namespace Ownership Evidence

| Rows | Ownership evidence | Remaining row scope |
|---|---|---|
| 56 `clone`, 57 `fork`, 58 `vfork`, 435 `clone3` | Clone core inherits one retained `TaskNamespaceSnapshot`; requested non-network `CLONE_NEW*` replacements allocate canonical `namespace_identity::NamespaceRef` owners, mount namespaces retain `vfs::mntns::MntNamespaceRef`, and `CLONE_NEWPID` publishes exact child/ancestor PID mappings only after fallible setup. `clone_without_new_flags_inherits_exact_owners`, `clone_replaces_every_supported_nonnetwork_owner_and_final_release_drops_them`, and scheduler nested-PID tests prove inheritance, isolation, rollback, PID-for-children transition, and weak ancestor identity behavior. B1257 rejects `CLONE_VFORK` without required `CLONE_VM` and arms the child's vfork completion before making it schedulable, so a fast exec/exit cannot lose the parent rendezvous. | Existing `PARTIAL` gaps remain; `CLONE_NEWTIME` returns `EINVAL` pending e.iv clock semantics. |
| 60 `exit`, 61 `wait4`, 62 `kill`, 109 `setpgid`, 110 `getppid` | Tasks own exact PID namespace ancestry; PID lookup mappings retain weak namespace identities and cannot retarget after final owner drop. `mark_done_releases_all_nonnetwork_membership_before_zombie` observes the namespace finalizer while the task is still `Runnable`, then proves `Zombie` publication and empty task/mount namespace snapshots; wait/kill/pgrp/parent lookups therefore use retained task or zombie PID snapshots without extending task namespace membership. | Ownership evidence does not close the rows' existing wait, signal, process-group, parent, permission, or errno-order audits. |
| 63 `uname`, 170 `sethostname`, 171 `setdomainname` | UTS names are keyed by the exact canonical UTS owner, not a numeric side table; the owner finalizer removes state. `clones_share_one_owner_state`, `exact_owners_are_isolated`, `owner_kind_and_init_are_validated`, `final_owner_drop_removes_state`, and nsfd-only UTS retention prove shared state, isolation, validation, and final cleanup. | F742 closed the syscall side: `uts_policy::check_uts_set` fixes the EPERM-before-EINVAL order, the `int len` truncation and the `__NEW_UTS_LEN` window; `ns_capable(uts_ns->user_ns, CAP_SYS_ADMIN)` replaces the flat effective-set test; the store is verbatim with the newline trim moved to the procfs hook; and `uname(2)` reports the stored nodename verbatim instead of substituting `(none)` for an empty one. |
| 29-31 and 64-71 SysV IPC | `ipc::IpcOwner` validates and retains the task's exact canonical IPC `NamespaceRef`; sem, msg, shm, and POSIX-mqueue stores key by that owner's `NamespaceId`, with finalizers reaping owner state. `typed_owner_rejects_non_ipc_identity_without_substitution` and `exact_owner_controls_sharing_isolation_and_final_cleanup` prove no fallback owner, same-owner sharing, cross-owner isolation, and final cleanup. | Statuses remain unchanged; B865 establishes namespace ownership only, not unlisted syscall semantics. |
| 165 `mount`, 166 `umount2` | Syscall entry snapshots the exact caller `MntNamespaceRef`; reservations, graft/copy mutations, commit/abort transactions, procfs open snapshots, and namespace live indexes retain canonical owners. `mntns_ownership` stress proves owner retention through success and rollback; `open_tree_mntns_ownership` proves a detached tree retains owner A and atomically rebinds every node to owner B on attach. | Existing mount-flag, filesystem, `MNT_EXPIRE`, `MNT_FORCE`, propagation, and errno-order gaps remain. |
| 272 `unshare` | Real `sys_unshare` hosted entry tests prove all supported non-network owner replacements, PID-for-children behavior, second pending PID `EINVAL`, rollback without owner leak, and `ESRCH` after terminal task namespace release. B886 also proves `CLONE_FILES` publishes a distinct table while retaining shared file descriptions and isolates subsequent close. F742 adds real `sys_unshare` coverage for the capability gate (per-flag EPERM without CAP_SYS_ADMIN, `CLONE_NEWUSER` alone exempt, FILES/FS/SYSVSEM uncapped), the single-threaded thread-flag no-ops, unknown-bit `EINVAL` with no mutation, the SEM_UNDO detach through the real `ipc` undo list, and cgroup-namespace root pinning. `CLONE_NEWTIME` clones monotonic/boottime offsets into a for-children namespace. | F742 added the missing `ns_capable(user_ns, CAP_SYS_ADMIN)` gate over the whole namespace set (with Linux's `CLONE_NEWUSER`-alone exemption), the three `check_unshare_flags` thread rungs, the `CLONE_SYSVSEM`/`CLONE_NEWIPC` SEM_UNDO detach, and real `copy_cgroup_ns` root pinning behind `CLONE_NEWCGROUP`. Rootless `CLONE_NEWUSER` capability inheritance waits on per-user-namespace credential scoping in `sched::cred` (`docs/27`). |
| 308 `setns` | `NsInode` retains a typed exact owner; `setns_from_fd` pins one `Arc<File>` before close/reuse. Per-kind tests cover cgroup, IPC, PID, PID-for-children, user, UTS, mount, and TIME nsfds: fd reuse cannot retarget the pin, final close/drop removes weak live indexes and owner state, and incomplete TIME install returns `EINVAL` without owner mutation. | Existing pidfd multi-namespace, permission, commit-order, and e.iv TIME semantics remain. |
| 470 `listns` | B867 replaces task/inode discovery with one canonical eight-kind monotonic ID registry and atomic active global/per-kind/direct-owner indexes. Separate active references and lifetime pins make nsfd/listns snapshots retain allocation without extending activity; mount/network concrete identities and owner activity cascades use the same registry. Extension/reserved validation, visibility, owner filtering, nsfd-only entries, cursor pagination, empty pages, per-element faults, and snapshot/final-drop schedules pass. | `IMPL`; follow-on work may broaden differential stress but no known row contract is intentionally omitted. |

## B884 AF_PACKET Differential Evidence

| Rows | Implemented evidence | Remaining row scope |
|---|---|---|
| 9 `mmap`, 41 `socket`, 44 `sendto`, 45 `recvfrom`, 54 `setsockopt`, 55 `getsockopt` | One portable GNU/glibc probe now exercises every implemented packet set/get option, malformed lengths and pointers, V1/V2/V3 RX, TX, exact/short/offset/private/combined mappings, all fanout modes, pressure/statistics, poll transitions, descriptor close, forked mappings, and mapped-ring teardown. The 79-record Linux oracle is byte-identical across three runs; both GNU targets compile with native glibc interpreters. Opt-in rootfs injection and an early root service preserve ordered UART records before unrelated late-boot failures. | B885-B965 close every recorded AF_PACKET differential mismatch and expand the matrix to 95 byte-identical records on both architectures. B886 fixes malformed generic `SOL_SOCKET` constant patterns exposed by the N07.10.10 integrated gate; final clean x86 and ARM smoke pass without D-Bus broker or launcher failure. |
| 7 `poll`, 23 `select`, 232 `epoll_wait`, 270 `pselect6`, 271 `ppoll`, 281 `epoll_pwait`, 441 `epoll_pwait2` | Real glibc `poll` probes prove ordinary AF_PACKET initial/readable/drained/writable transitions and TX-ring kick completion on Linux and Oxide. B965 adds direct epoll coverage for all TX states, V3 retirement, and controlled blocked-receive close. | Device-down readiness, exact epoll ready-list semantics, and non-AF_PACKET row-specific ABI/error-ordering gaps remain. |

## B885 AF_PACKET Getsockopt Evidence

| Rows | Implemented evidence | Remaining row scope |
|---|---|---|
| 55 `getsockopt` | `SOL_PACKET` option dispatch now resolves one fixed-size kernel value before one common Linux-ordered copyout: clamp value-result length, write `optlen`, then write `optval`. Unsupported options return `ENOPROTOOPT` before either output changes; statistics still reset before a later copy fault. The 79-record x86 GNU/glibc differential removes all three prior getsockopt differences. Hosted syscalls 121/121 and x86_64/aarch64 kernel builds pass. | Row remains `PARTIAL`: full non-packet option coverage, capability/LSM behavior, remaining family readback, namespace/device teardown, and wider direct syscall differential coverage remain. N07.10 retains ring metadata/V3 defects and the aarch64/integrated completion gates. |

## B887 AF_PACKET V3 Private-Width Evidence

| Rows | Implemented evidence | Remaining row scope |
|---|---|---|
| 9 `mmap`, 54 `setsockopt` | Linux 6.19 source and host runtime disprove the queued V3 private-offset widening: the kernel validates the full `u32` request but stores `blk_sizeof_priv` as `unsigned short`. A request of 65,536 is accepted and exposes private and first-packet offsets of 48 on Linux and Oxide. Hosted boundary tests cover 65,535/65,536/65,537 plus full-width validation failures; the portable 80-record GNU/glibc differential matches this record exactly. Hosted net passes 854/854 and both GNU targets compile. | Existing non-AF_PACKET mmap and setsockopt row gaps remain. N07.10 retains loopback packet classification, duplicate V3 publication, expanded differential cases, aarch64 execution, and integrated gates. |

## B894 AF_PACKET Fanout Semantics Evidence

| Row | Evidence | Remaining |
|---|---|---|
| 41 `socket`, 54 `setsockopt` | Packet-origin output suppresses its complete fanout group before selection while ordinary sockets suppress only the origin. The group hook alone applies `PACKET_FANOUT_FLAG_IGNORE_OUTGOING`; a selected member's `PACKET_IGNORE_OUTGOING` does not suppress fanout delivery. Final release uses Linux swap-delete ordering. Packet-ring replacement validates before unlink, then unlinks and appends a running member under one membership/delivery lock order; rejected changes preserve member order. Hosted tests cover LB, CPU, QM, CBPF, EBPF, close order, BPF retention, successful and rejected ring replacement, group suppression, and ordinary observers. Four new GNU/glibc records (`origin_suppresses_group`, `member_ignore_not_group_flag`, `group_ignore_outgoing`, `close_swap_delete`) match host Linux exactly in the x86 84-record differential. Hosted net passes 860/860 and both kernel targets build. | Existing non-AF_PACKET socket and setsockopt row gaps remain. N07.10 retains queue charging, hardware timestamps, loopback classification, duplicate V3 publication, expanded differential cases, aarch64 execution, and integrated gates. |

## B903 AF_PACKET TX Poll Evidence

| Syscall rows | Evidence added | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| 7 `poll`, 23 `select`, 232 `epoll_wait`, 270 `pselect6`, 271 `ppoll`, 281 `epoll_pwait`, 441 `epoll_pwait2` | The canonical AF_PACKET socket poll mask preserves generic datagram `POLL_OUT` readiness for available, `SEND_REQUEST`, `SENDING`, and `WRONG_FORMAT` TX-ring states. TX-ring status notifications are keyed to `POLL_OUT`, so read-only subscribers are not spuriously woken. Hosted tests cover every state, a real completion, and subscriber filtering. The GNU/glibc probe obtains kernel-generated `WRONG_FORMAT` from an invalid TX offset, observes readiness, repairs and republishes the frame, and completes it. The full TX record matches host Linux byte-for-byte in the x86 84-record differential. Focused TX tests pass 11/11, full net passes 860/860, both GNU targets compile, and x86_64/aarch64 kernel builds pass. | Row statuses are unchanged. Broader send-buffer pressure, device-down readiness, exact epoll ready-list semantics, and non-AF_PACKET row-specific ABI/error-ordering gaps remain. B948 removes the final AF_PACKET differential mismatches. |

## B925 AF_PACKET Queue Truesize Evidence

| Syscall rows | Evidence added | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| 41 `socket`, 45 `recvfrom`, 47 `recvmsg`, 54 `setsockopt`, 55 `getsockopt`, 299 `recvmmsg` | The canonical non-ring AF_PACKET queue retains Linux 6.19 64-bit skb allocation-class charge instead of Rust object/payload size. Admission tests current rmem before charging, so the frame crossing `SO_RCVBUF` is accepted and the next frame drops; dequeue releases the exact retained charge. Copy fallback and fanout rollover consume the same charge model. Hosted tests cover exact linear/paged classes, crossing admission, pressure clearing, and destructive packet/drop statistics. A GNU/glibc probe with effective `SO_RCVBUF=4096` proves Linux and Oxide both accept five 64-byte frames and drop the sixth. Full net passes 861/861, both GNU targets compile, both kernel targets build, and the x86 85-record differential differs only in the three existing N07.10.8 RX-ring records. | Row statuses remain unchanged. Broader `SO_RCVBUF` semantics, ring publication, security hooks, and non-AF_PACKET row audits remain. B943 closes production timestamp ingress; B948 closes the recorded RX-ring mismatches. |

## B943 AF_PACKET Receive Timestamp Evidence

| Syscall rows | Evidence added | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| 9 `mmap`, 41 `socket`, 54 `setsockopt` | Linux-netdev skb software and raw-hardware receive timestamps now flow into canonical packet metadata. AF_PACKET keeps driver timestamps separate from mandatory realtime fallback, applies Linux hardware-then-software source precedence, and emits no source status bit when no requested source exists. Virtio-net 1.2 has no receive timestamp field and correctly reports no hardware source. GNU/glibc V1/V2/V3 raw-hardware-without-source records match host Linux byte-for-byte in the x86 88-record differential. Hosted modules pass 14/14, full net passes 861/861, virtio metadata passes 1/1, both GNU targets compile, and both kernel targets build. | Row statuses remain unchanged. Device-specific timestamp production, timestamp configuration ioctls, non-AF_PACKET option coverage, and row-specific ABI/error-ordering gaps remain. B948 removes the final AF_PACKET differential mismatches. |

## B948 AF_PACKET Loopback Publication Evidence

| Syscall rows | Evidence added | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| 9 `mmap`, 41 `socket`, 44 `sendto`, 45 `recvfrom`, 47 `recvmsg`, 299 `recvmmsg` | Packet buffers retain a canonical MAC-header marker across loopback transmit. Raw loopback sends publish one synchronous receive view classified against the device broadcast address while preserving the stripped L3 queue view. Outgoing taps dispatch only through `ETH_P_ALL` sockets and groups before fanout selection, matching Linux `ptype_all` ordering. Protocol-bound V1/V2/V3 rings report multicast packet type 2 and one V3 publication; `ETH_P_ALL` retains distinct outgoing and receive taps. Hosted regressions cover V3 count/classification and hidden-outgoing LB selector state. Full net passes 863/863, both kernel targets build, and all 88 x86 GNU/glibc differential records match host Linux byte-for-byte. | Row statuses remain unchanged. B965 closes broader offload, close-race, mapping, and blocked-I/O evidence; non-AF_PACKET row-specific ABI/error-ordering gaps remain. |

It covers plain checksum completion, TCPv4 with and without ECN, TCPv6, legacy UDP/UFO, UDP_L4/USO, invalid ECN-only and zero-GSO-size requests, concurrent fanout-member close, split and partial unmap with fork lifetime, `mremap` lifetime, and close while blocked receive with a pre-opened sender that excludes descriptor-reuse races.
